swccg-discussion: Re: Unfortunatly...Having said that, I seem to remember a straw poll suggesting that 

most people played SWCCG because they were huge SW fans, not usually 

because it was a CCG. (After all, if you believe the press, it's a 

crap game where the only decent cards are rares...) Hence, just like 

most gaming trends, Magic could well fade and die. People are 

unlikely to remain particularly faithful to a specific genre - if 

WOTC started producing low quality cards (as if ;) then its players 

may well swap to Middle-Earth or some similar CCG, or probably go 

back to RPGs and Monopoly for all I know.

However, unless something very strange happens, all those subscribing 

to this group are very likely to be SW fans for life, and will quite 

likely support the game for longer than a genre they had little 

affinity for. Which is not to say that we'd be more gullible and 

continue buying substandard cards but we're certainly a lot less 

likely to get bored with the genre.

As for the computer graphics thing, don't forget that a sizable 

amount of the SE films will be CG. For people like Mara Jade (who, I 

would imagine would not be seen until about 2001, and only then if 

Lucas doesn't come up with the goods on time for the new films) they 

could very easily get an actress,

Dom.

From: SMITH Michael
Subject: Re: Dark Diss/Furry Fury
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/19991223225713/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/6.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Dark Diss/Furry [address removed] wrote:

I haven't been able to find out how these cards work. When it says

"Draw two destiny. Subtract that amount from opponenets attrition and

total power." does the first destiny subtract from the attrition, and

the second from the power, or do you add the total of the two destinies

and subtact first from the attrition and the whatever fraction of the

total destiny left over subtracts from total power??

=========

Whenever you draw multiple destiny, you add the individual draws to

arrive at a single destiny value. Let X be your total destiny value.

Your opponent has cards in a battle with a total power of P and then

draws battle destiny D. His/her attrition is D and his/her total power

is P+D. You get to subtract X from both those amounts. Thus, his/her

attrition is now D-X and his/her total power is P+D-X. Note that there

is a general rule that values can never fall below 0.

Mike Smith

http://www.geocities.com/TimesSquare/6375/index.html

Mike's UNOFFICIAL SWCCG Rules and Lists Page

===== swccg-discussion message #11 (egroups) =====
Date: 7:30 AM

From: Francis Lalumiere
Subject: Dark Dissension and Furry Fury
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/19991223034402/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/11.html?

swccg-discussion: Dark Dissension and Furry FuryBiggs wrote:

>I haven't been able to find out how these cards work. When it says "Draw two
>destiny. Subtract that amount from opponenets attrition and total power."
>does the first destiny subtract from the attrition, and the second from the
>power, or do you add the total of the two destinies and subtact first from
>the attrition and the whatever fraction of the total destiny left over
>subtracts from total power??

 You substract the amount from both: attrition _and_ total power.
Let's use an example where Dark Dissension is used in a battle against two
Imperials each with ability >2 (among other characters).

Dark Side has total power of 12.
Light Side has total power of 8

Dark Side draws battle destiny: 4
Light Side draws the two destinies for Dark Dissension: total of 6.
Light Side draws battle destiny: 3

Dark Side total power: 12 + 4(battle destiny) - 6(Dark Dis.) = 10
Dark Side attrition: 4 - 6(Dark Dis.) = 0 (cannot fall below zero)

Light Side total power: 8 + 3(battle destiny) = 11
Light Side attrition: 3

 Thus the Light Side wins, inflicting a total battle damage of 1 to
the Dark Side. But the Dark Side must also forfeit 3 to attrition.
No loss whatsoever for the Light Side.

 Francis Lalumiere - Red 23

===== swccg-discussion message #2578 (egroups) =====
Date: 5:08 AM

Sat
1
Feb 97
From: Scott White
Subject: OT: Movie Premier report Friday Night Boston Are
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000110072708/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/2578.html?

swccg-discussion: OT: Movie Premier report Friday Night Boston AreI showed up a 5:15 pm. to my local General Cinema theater 12 screen 
cinema. The manager allowed me to setup my display of cards and
Jedi packs on a small table right where the line forms for the movie.
By the time I got there, lines were already forming for the 3 screens
they showed the movie on. The first two screens were sold out long
before I got there with reports of over 300 tickets sold by noon time.
As soon as I put the cards out there was immediate interest! Many
kids and adults were looking at the cards and taking Jedi packs and
single common cards. As the line formed out of the theater and 
into the night air...I took hand fulls of commons and Jedi packs and
went up and down the line giving cards away and telling them about 
Decipher's game. People ate the cards up like candy!!! By the time
I got into my seat to view the movie for myself, over 100 Jedi packs
and 500 common cards were handed out. It was neat, sitting in my
seat waiting for the movie to start and looking around and seeing kids 
and adults with Decipher cards in their hands!

After the movie, I went out and got another 500 cards out and did
the same thing for the second showing. I probably could have
easily handed out 2000-3000 cards if I had that many to give! But,
even 1000 cards and 100 jedi packs might just bring a few people
to the game. If it does...then I feel it was worth the effort. 

By the time I left...lines were forming again out of the theater and
into the parking lot! I have NEVER seen this theater so mobbed by
people! The movie was drawing people of all ages. Teens were 
a huge part of the audience. Most of these never seeing the movie
on the big screen. Some of my tournament attenders were actually
there to see the movie again. One mother took her kid out of 
school and made a special day of it for him.

All I can say...is I had goose bumps throughout the movie... especially
at the new scenes and the enhanced explosions and ship scenes. 
Though I have seen the movie dozens of times, the new scenes made
it fresh and interesting. You could hear people in the audience going
'ooooh...that's new' all the time! My girls enjoyed the movie ages
4-14 and it was neat that in a way, we were able to share a piece of
my teen years together in a way parents don't get too very often!

Oh yeah...did I say I liked...no...enjoyed...no...LOVED the movie!
If this does not give a boost to the the game...then nothing will!

--May the force be with you --Scott/Red 21

Subject: Re: Moving Aritllery Weapons.
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000110140155/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/2582.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Moving Aritllery Weapons.> Some weapons such as the E-web Blaster and
> the Medium Repeating Blaster can be carried
> by two warriors. But how else can they be
> moved?
> 
> My older son and I disucssed this the other
> day and came up with some 'house rules' for
> moving aritllery weapons.
> 
> For example LS has two warrirors and the MRB
> at an exterior site (say the 3rd marker). 
> LS wants the warriors and the MRB to get to
> Tatooine to defend Lars Moisture Farm. Can
> the warriors and the MRB shuttle up to a
> corvette and then be moved to Tatooine? We
> decided yes, but the MRB occupied a
> 'passenger' slot on the corvette.
> 

Why? It's not necessary. Just consider the weapon to be a weapon or 
a device with it's mass distributed among two warriors. Two warriors, 
each with a blaster, don't take up any extra slots in a ship. 
Therefore, two warriors, sans blasters but carrying an E-Web wouldn't 
take up anymore space.

From: Martin Arlt [address removed]
Subject: Cards Decipher Forgot
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20010508194123/http://groups.yahoo.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/message/2595

After seeing the SE (sorry Juz), I've been thinking about some cards that
Decipher didn't make from A New Hope. Of course, since there will be a SE
expansion, Decipher has a second chance at making these cards, even though
they're not new material.

Here are 4 cards that weren't made. Can anyone else think of others?

-Binders/Handcuffs. There's been a lot of discussion recently about
expanding/augmenting the capture rules. The binders they put on Chewie seem
like a good way to do this.

-"Use the Force, Luke." Come on! If nothing else, this should have been an
Attack-Run bonus card.

-Imperial Officer. I don't know his name, but he comes into the DS
conference room to tell Tarkin that Dantooine is deserted. He has a clear
screen shot and has lines. Other characters have been given cards for less
than this.

-Rebel Troop Transport. These are the small vehicles used on Yavin 4 and
Hoth which transported Rebel pilots to their ships. We had some cool card
ideas for this a few months ago. 

Martin.............

From: Ed ('You May E-mail When Read
Subject: Re: creature question
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000108071251/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/4514.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: creature questionAt 04:49 AM 3/1/97 +0100, you wrote:
>Hello,
>
>Thank you very much for your reply.

no prob. :)

>So in fact a creature attack is sort of a new phase. It comes before the
>battlephase and is not considered a battle. So you can't use weapons.
>(even if the character being atacked carries one?). Can you use for
>exemple 'Warrior Courage' when a creature attacks you?

basically yeah... it still falls under the attack phase, but its timing
happens BEFORE normal battle (I had asked mark about this LONG ago when
tony & i ran into some timing problems involving his & my characters at a
site with a dianoga).

No, characters can't use weapons when a creature attacks (even if they are
carrying one). They can use them if THEY attack a creature however.

And finally, no, things that add destiny (like Warrior's Courage) cannot be
used with creatures. When a creature attacks it's called a 'creature
attack" (source: R2 Sensor Array), not a "creature battle".

confused yet? :)

"The Price of Plenty: Dagobah
 Release Pushed to Mid-April

 Carol Wisely
 VP Marketing 

 What's the most popular single movie or series of
 movies in history? The envelope please....and any
 guesses other than Star Wars don't count. What's the
 best time ever to be a Star Wars fan? The only answer
 is, "Now!" 

 The ripple effects of the Star Wars movies phenomenon
 is being felt around the universe. Well, the media and
 marketing universe, anyway, and the universe made up of
 people who love Star Wars. Sometimes the effects are
 felt in unexpected ways. For instance, there's the down
 side, a not-so-down side, to the flood of cool Star
 Wars merchandise and paraphernalia that has accompanied
 the movies' re-release. 

 Thousands of neat things based on Star Wars must be
 reviewed by the teams at Lucasfilm. A bottleneck has
 formed. The Lucasfilm teams are working feverishly, and
 miracles are happening daily.... But we know that one
 effect of their tremendous workload is that Dagobah
 will not be reviewed in time for a March release. To be
 safe, and conservative, we offer a Dagobah release date
 of mid-April. We wouldn't dare even to guess as to the
 exact day! 

 This is what we know, and this is all that we know at
 the moment. As any news of Dagobah's release is
 learned, it will be shared, just as this has been. Till
 then, stay tuned to the Newsflash."

===== swccg-discussion message #4519 (egroups) =====
Date: 7:48 AM

Sat
1
Mar 97
From: mark-
Subject: Re: Firing Weopons
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000108152405/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/4519.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Firing Weopons> I am relatively new to the game, so this may be a stupid question. But, I
> was wondering when you could fire a weopon. Do you have to initiate (or be
> involved in) a battle before you can fire? The rulebook states that you
> have to fire at a target at the same location. However, some cards allow you
> to fire at targets at adjacent sights. I realize that these cards would
> have presedence over the rules - but can you do this outside of a battle?
> Or, do I have to declare a battle at one sight before firing weopons located
> at one of it's adjacent sights?

You can only fire a weapon during the battle phase (phase right after 
the "Just initiated" phase). There are a few cards that allow you to 
fire weapons during the Control Phase like Sniper (DS) and Sorry 
About The Mess (LS), plus others. The cards that allowed you to 
target at adjacent sites, then you only need battle at either the 
site where the weapon is (allowing you to fire at an adjacent site) 
OR battle at the site you would like to fire IN TO, with possibly no 
opposing presence at the site of the weapon. 

Make sense?

 Mark T., Decipher Net Rep

===== swccg-discussion message #4525 (yahoo) =====
Date: Sat Mar 1, 1997  10:17
am
From: [address removed]
Subject: Re: Commander Luke Question
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20010508192608/http://groups.yahoo.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/message/4525

> During a tournament is the deployment
> restriction on Cmdr. Luke "deploy only on
> Hoth" taken to mean that he can only deploy
> at a Hoth site? Can can he be deployed
> directly onto a speeder (or other vehicle)
> on Hoth? Can he be deployed onto a starship
> at the Hoth system?

Yes, you can deploy him directly onto a vehicle at a Hoth Site (with 
Icons or previous presence of course) and, you may deploy Cmmdr. Luke 
onto a starship at the Hoth System. Any card that has a deployment 
restriction of a planet is still considered deploying to that 
location if it is to an orbiting ship.

===== swccg-discussion message #7170 (egroups) =====
Date: 2:10 AM

Tue
1
Apr 97
From: Patience Frederiksen
Subject: Re: Game Mechanics (was Re: CI: Concussion Missil
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000111075853/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/7170.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Game Mechanics (was Re: CI: Concussion Missil> I disagree here. Battle Power is based on the inherent ability of a

> vehicle/character/whatever to do damage during that battle (with standard

> "built-in" weaponry, fists, sticks and stones, or maybe even ramming). 

> It has (or *should* have) little to do with surviving to that phase.

Bit of a typo on my part-battle power is *generated by* the reasons 

stated above, but it only has significance if it's still there at the end 

of the battle. You argue that once one goes to the trouble to deploy 

something with power, it has every right to add that power to your side 

in a battle right up to the end of a battle in which you decide its 

usefulness has come to an end or it's been hit by a weapon. I tend to 

disagree with that viewpoint, as this is a combat game and while *most* 

weapons can conceivably be survived in some form, a few (like the 

Superlaser) aren't even capable of inflicting survivable damage to their 

targets-assuming their masters are even gracious enough to try to do so.

The points you raise about real-time simulation of the game are valid,

but I tend to think of the DS II in Jedi as I write this: the Star 

Cruisers it destroys certainly do damage before their destruction, but 

that's represented in firing their weapons and drawing battle destiny 

contingent on its passengers (i.e, Skywalkers or Don't Get Cocky.) A 

Superlasered ship should certainly have the chance to do that level of 

damage, but I can't see the remnants of the ships and their pilots 

amounting to anything else in the course of that battle. Maybe we should 

total power before firing weapons, declaring the total damaging potential 

of our forces before seeing them take attrition/carbon scoring/etc. This 

would seem more logical in some ways than our current system of combat.

===== swccg-discussion message #7172 (egroups) =====
Date: 2:58 AM

From: mark-
Subject: April First Net Cleaning
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000111092837/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/7172.html?

swccg-discussion: April First Net Cleaning*** Attention ***

It's that time again!

As many of you know, each leap year the internet must be shut down for
24 hours in order to allow us to clean it. The cleaning process,
which eliminates dead email and inactive ftp, www and gopher sites,
allows for a better-working and faster internet.

This year, the cleaning process will take place from 12:01 a.m. EST on
April 1 until 12:01 a.m. EST on April 2. During that 24-hour period,
five powerful internet-crawling robots situated around the world will
search the internet and delete any data that they find.

In order to protect your valuable data from deletion we ask that you
do the following:

1. Disconnect all terminals and local area networks from their
internet connections.

2. Shut down all internet servers, or disconnect them from the
internet.

3. Disconnect all disks and hardrives from any connections to the
internet.

4. Refrain from connecting any computer to the internet in any way.
We understand the inconvenience that this may cause some internet
users, and we apologize. However, we are certain that any
inconveniences will be more than made up for by the increased speed
and efficiency of the internet, once it has been cleared of electronic
flotsam and jetsam.

We thank you for your cooperation.

 Mark T., Decipher Net Rep

'Dagobah'. Let's start with Bill Clinton: Mr. President, what are your
impressions of the game."

[Bill, dressed in his typical blue "power" suit] "Well, first I'd like to
start off by saying thank you for having me on this show and thanks go to
the voters. Without their help I would be sitting right now at McDonalds
with Hillary and Chelsea." [leans over to a SS agent, whispering] "Speaking
of which, make sure you remind me to swing by the drive-thru when we are
done here" [back to camera] "As you know, Decipher makes great games and I
can be 100 percent certain that this new expansion will excede all
expectations. It is to the employees at Decipher that my heart goes out to
today."

[newscaster] "Thank you, Mr. President. Now on to ex-senator Mr. Bob Dole"

[Bob, with that perpetual frown look] "Bob Dole doesn't play silly games
that these kids today play. Back in Bob Dole's day we used to beat each
other about the head with sticks the size of those little Mexican chuhuahua
dogs. That's what this country needs again are kids who know the TRUE
meaning of fun without all of this false violence that our society provides
for them! Why, I fought in World War II and bounced a grenade off a tree
just to have it explode by me. You want excite, well Bob Dole has news for
you. But first Bob Dole needs his afternoon nap." [promptly falls asleep
at his podium]

[newscaster] "There you have it, folks. And now for our final commentator
of the day, Mr. Ross Perot"

[camera shot of cowboy hat just barely above the top of the podium, sound
of footsteps on a step ladder, and Ross pops up from behind] "How-doo,
folks. Lemme tell ya something... Decipher knows how to do great games,
yes they do. If you can't take my word for it, maybe you can take a horse
to water and have him eat peanut butter next to the pole-cat. Heh heh.
But seriously folks, I'm here today to illustrate my point with these pie
charts I brought with me... Now if you take a look at--"

[Bill] "Ohh, blow it out your ear, you Yoda reject. The American public
is tired of your charts and demographics!"

[Bob, waking up] "...and another thing, Bob Dole does not LIKE pineapple!"
[falls back to sleep]

[Ross] "Well, that's fine & dandy for you, Billy-boy, but a stick of celery
in the hand is worth a penny earned for a rainy day..."

[arguments continue as the camera pans back to the newscaster]

"So as you can see, ladies and gentleman, 2 out of 3 candidates don't know
what the hell they are talking about. This has been DNN, where the art of
great games comes true."

[fade out]

swccg-discussion: Test #2 and Timing Hypothesis> ONLY CATCH HERE IS:

> This relies upon the NEW (impending *sigh*) timing rules. Under the old

> rules, your opponent may be able to declare a move in response to your

> move, and it would still be the start of the turn (conditions valid at

> declaration).

> i.e. Declare Apprentice move

> Opponent declares test move (legally)

> Apprentice move resolves.

> Test move resolves. It's gone again.

>

> This is not an argument for errating the test, it is just an argument

foir

> releasing the timing rules. PLEEEAAASSSEEE!!!!!

>

> Cheers,

> Juz

Well Juz, I would say that you couldn't move the Test as a react, since

react necessarily would indicate that it wasn't the beginning of the turn,

even under the old timing rules, right? Once the declaration of the move

is made, the 'beginning' is over. Anyways, I'd like to see the new timing

rules released as well.

- - - - - - - -----------------------

Careful to note that there is a difference between response and react

(react carries a pretty specific game function).

I think this is legal under existing rules because I believe that the any

previous phase/action (in this case, movement phase) is valid until another

action _resolves_.

Anyway, it's all academic as the FAQ is about to be released anyway......

...... I can feel it ......

...... right....?

(lots of still working on it's, but has anyone seen a DATE given for the

FAQ? )

From: justin.pake-
Subject: Counting my piles
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000105151332/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/10345.html?

swccg-discussion: Counting my piles}Alright, Current rulings indicate that one can count their reserve deck at

}anytime.

}This would allow one to see how close an insterted card would be to the

top

}of their pile. Could one count, activate enough to avoid the card and

then

}at some point Bith Shuffle so as to effectively push the card farther

down?

}

I'm afraid that has changed. The new tournament guidelines under code of

conduct section 2 says you may not count your reserve, used or lost piles

unless you have a specific card that allows you to do so. And that card

must

be shown before you count the pile. I don't believe that the shuffle cards

allow you to count the pile being shuffled.

Sorry

Dave

- - - - - - - -------------------------

Bill,

For reference, this is also mentioned in the now Official SWCCG Rulebook

(ESB 2P game).

===== swccg-discussion message #10352 (egroups) =====
Date: 1:09 AM

Thu
1
May 97
From: justin.pake-
Subject: CPMMENT: Actions in activation
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000106033019/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/10352.html?

swccg-discussion: CPMMENT: Actions in activationFrom: Jason Robinette [address removed]

Date: Wed, 30 Apr 1997 15:52:40 -0500

Subject: Question

Can any game actions happen in the activation phase that don't cancel or

modify Force activation?

Just need a clarification on this one.

- - - - - - - ----------------

Under current timing rules, yes. You can do just about anything just about

anytime.

Under impending (!!!!) timing rules, this is a testy one.

The original draft of the new timing rules suggested that the phase you are

in should be treated like "a card of that name in play". Thus everyone

gets the chance to respond to it, then respond to those responses etc. Here

a response is a direct modify/cancel of the top card in the "action pile"

as it were.

A comment I made when reveiwing the FAQ was that if every action was a

reponse to another action, or, at the top level, a specific response to a

phase, this left no place to play those actions which have no specific

timing (i.e. they don't say, During Your X Phase...).

Sandy agreed, and was working on it.

Cheers,

Juz

Hi all,

I've got a question about starships that have landed at sites.

Now according to the Hoth rules, they don't have power or

the ability to use game text. But I'm wondering, do they

create presence so that you can battle them?

For example, say I have Han on the Falcon at the Big one: Cave.

Can the DS deploy Vader there and attack to have a power of

6 vs. 0? And if so, does Han at least get a draw because

he's on the Falcon?

Brett

Here's the last thing I heard on this snipped form my Mark's Rulings

Archive:

=-=-=-

> If a piloted starfighter is landed at an exterior site, the starfighter

has

> power = 0, the pilot cannot contribute personal power to a battle at that

> location, the ship (and pilot) can be forfeited to cover battle losses,

and

> the ship and pilot has presence (so can control, force drain, block force

> drain, attack, and be attacked).

>

> Question: Does the pilot (permanent or otherwise) contribute ability to a

> battle at that location?

>

No, they do not.

=-=-=-

From: motti1-
Subject: Griping about Dagobah.
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000226111838/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/13351.html?

swccg-discussion: Griping about Dagobah.I just played in a tournament this weekend and you know something it just

wasn't fun. Dagobah has spawned a whole lot of decks that aren't fun to play

against. I don't mean just losing to them but just that they require no

amount of thought, they make you put in a hundred counter cards, and never is

there any amount of fun interaction between players. I am talking about the

new insert decks, the new Asteroid Sanctuary decks(Even with the errata it is

a stupid card), the sit on Dagobah and force drain decks. I never have an

ounce of fun playing these decks. Even though I have used each one of them I

don't think they are fun to create or play with. With the hundred of good

things that Dagobah has brought to the game it also added these boring

elements. What they have in common is that the two opposing players never

get to interact, inserting your card into the opponents reserve isn't what I

call clever game play. What happened to the epic space conflicts and the

ground battles. What happened to characters interacting? Where are the cool

combos? I miss the days of deploying my characters to BATTLE my opponent.

 Hopefully cloud city will bring that back or it will get pretty boring

seeing who has the better characterless insert deck.

I know that several of you will write in and say that these characterless

decks have strategy behind them and are fun to play. Maybe you were able to

beat the world champion with one and think that your deck building skills

match his. Why don't you write how much time you put into deck construction

and how much thought you put into it? I am sure that it would amount to

about 20 minutes. That is how long it takes to put together one of these

decks and how little strategy goes into it. Why don't you ask your opponent

how much fun he had playing your deck? The answer to this question is that

he will have had none. And for all of you who agree with me why don't you

write into this list-serv instead of sending me personal e-mail so maybe we

can have a counter card in Cloud City. Heck every card like AFA, 3720 to 1,

AS, and the rest should have some sort of defence. 

On a personal note. Disarmed is immune to alter.

Thanks for reading all you wonderful card players.

===== swccg-discussion message #13367 (yahoo) =====
Date: Sun Jun 1, 1997  12:05
pm
From: "Gordon Pitt" [address removed]
Subject: Pack Story
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20010508201027/http://groups.yahoo.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/message/13367

To me this is kinda funny:

I've been collecting and playing SWCCG since the beginning and have till
Dagobah gotten all the sets twice. But for the longest time I've had alot
of trouble getting main characters. So when Dagobah came out I decided to
go to my local comic store and buy a pack of Dagobah (I later went and
bought a box but i had to wait for a friend to meet me later in the week to
buy it) I wanted to see these cards. So I bought one pack and some people
I know and play against were saying that they had been buying packs and
haven't got the mains (Yoda, Son of Skywalker and Executor) so I was
pretty sure I would get any as well at least cause the way my luck getting
them had been going for awhile. So I buy my one pack and the last card in
the pack I got was Yoda!! Man was I happy (other people in the store
weren't) so I decided to buy another pack and in that I got the Son of
Skywalker. I couldn't help myself but to laugh at my friends expenses. It
seems that my luck with Mains has improved and I'm happy for it. (In the
box I bought I got another Yoda, Son of Skywalker and the Executor) Just
thought I'd add my story.
********************************************************

May the Force Be With You !!!!

Gordon "KnightFyr" Pitt

CorSec11 in the NSWL

Tuxedo_K (IRC-#starwarsccg,#NSWL)

===== swccg-discussion message #16641 (egroups) =====
Date: 7:34 AM

Tue
1
Jul 97
From: Martin Arlt
Subject: Re: WB & BB
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000110195645/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/16641.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: WB & BB>Ok, I realize that Unlimited is WB and Limited is BB. But, are they the

>exact same cards only with different borders? Or do they have different

>pictures and text? I'd just really like to know before I chuck out a

>good portion of my hobby budget to get a box.

The pictures are the same. Some of the cards have had their game text

fixed in the WB edition, most notably Electrobinoculars. If you're looking

strictly for playing and not for collecting, I would go with the WB. They

might not look as cool as the BB, but the white edges hide the wear and

tear of playing much better (as the black gets scuffed, you can see the

white card stock underneath...doesn't happen with white on white). Also,

I've found you can get better deals on boxes of WB cards, since they're not

as collectible.

Of course, if you're interested in collecting in addition to gameplay (of

course), you would probably want to go with the BB.

Martin................

Subject: Nabrun Leids Strategies!
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000111033113/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/16647.html?

swccg-discussion: Nabrun Leids Strategies!OK, I see we're all up in arms (heh heh, arms, Nabrun? Never mind) about

Mr. Leids. The fact is, CHILL OUT! The card is not invincible! In fact it

is easy to stop. Yes, I'm annoyed that you can NL to the Executor and the

Death Star, but did you knonw you can use You Will Go To THe Dagobah System

to pop up on the Executor? So lets drop that complaint.

Now, the counters to NL. There are a few, but there is one I prefer. It's

called Space deck. Forget the ground. Unless you're packing 4 Vaders, the

DS always gets clobbered down there. Stay up in the vacuum with your big

honkin ships.

BUT, if you must play ground, Oota Goota? is excellent. Capturing them

means that: A) they cannot redeploy them (woo hoo!) and B) Poor little

Figrin and KFC are gonna get stomped next turn! So consider that before

forgetting about the card.

Also, Those Rebels Can't Escape Us is excellent. It cancels their NL, and

is USED. It also cancels a million other things too. An excellent card.

If you're tired of destiny adders as you mentioned earlier, try the gem of

the first set, BORING CONVERSATION ANYWAY. This card cancels ALL the

premiere team destiny adders, demotion, and a few others. Best of yet, it

can't be Controled! Consider these strategies. They're guarenteed to stop

NL.

Tom Fletcher

Other Side of the Bay, CA

Uhm... wouldn't a seeker move as one of YOUR characters, not the
enemy's?? I mean, you can't move Obi-Wan onto an AT-AT or shuttle him
to the bridge of the Avenger. So why can you do this with seekers?

I thought you could only board enemy vehicles/starships with undercover
spies? And that would be during the opponent's move phase. Since
seekers move during your own control phase I would definitely think that
they can't benefit from undercover spy abilities.

Think of it this way... the other player's shuttles only work during his
movement phase, and the doors to his enclosed vehicles only open during
his movement phase. That's why your undercover spies have to wait until
then to move.

===== swccg-discussion message #18785 (egroups) =====
Date: 3:18 PM

Thu
31
Jul 97
From: Martin Arlt
Subject: Re: SE Moved Back even further (kinda long)
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000111093246/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/18785.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: SE Moved Back even further (kinda long)First off, before I get flamed, I want to say that I think the quality of

the product is the most important thing. I'll gladly (well, I won't be

cheering) wait longer to make sure we get the best possible expansion.

Okay, now I have a few comments about the recent expansion release schedule

announcement.

Despite the fact that I'm really looking forward to the SE expansion, it

seems to make sense to put it after Tatooine. Other than the celebration

scenes, there are no major changes after the Tatooine segments in the SE.

Putting the expansion here gives Decipher a chance to put in any cards it

might have missed along the way.

The one thing that really bothers me in this announcement is the delay of

Cloud City. Sure, this gives me more time to save up for it, and there are

plenty of cards out already to keep us busy. Still, Decipher has long

fought a reputation as a company that can't deliver the goods on time.

Ever since SWCCG came out, there have been no real delays in releases.

Now, Decipher is finally getting the exposure and recognition it deserves

as a company you can rely on. This is the worst possible time to have a

late product! Interest in the game seems to be at a high, and people will

want that new product to continue their recreation of the trilogy (and I'm

not saying there need to be endless expansions to keep the game viable, but

everyone knows there's more story to make cards from). Dagobah came out in

April. If Cloud City comes out by October, that's a 6 month gap instead of

the originally-reported 3-4 month gap. I think this is going to hurt

Decipher's improving reputation. Obviously, I don't know the details in

the delay, but on the surface it looks like Decipher dropped the ball.

Of course I'll continue to support SWCCG, I'm not a whiner who will just

abandon ship. I'm just disappointed that I will have to wait longer for

what I expect will be one of the best expansions yet.

Martin.............

Perhaps you want to try to get your hands on an Alderaan (you get to
force drain +1 for each DS site you control) if you are planninhg to play
with all those HFB sites. Also, if your deck is based on blowing up
planets where the opponent makes a threat, get a R3-T6 and put it in the
centeral core. That will boost the HFB's hyperspeed to 2, so you can get
to the planets faster. There are a couple of cards you might not need,
and there could be some more you could add. You are dependant on blowing
he planets up, so you might want three more DS gunners, so you get them
out earlier (as well as the superlaser). Plus, in your stratedgy, you
said a lot about if your opponent deploys a rebel trooper. I have never
played a deck in a year and a half that used a rebel trooper!

===== swccg-discussion message #20812 (egroups) =====
Date: 11:20 AM

Sat
30
Aug 97
From: Maarten Logghe
Subject: Re: Questions (and more about the french cards)
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000308210533/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/20812.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Questions (and more about the french cards)Hello,

> 1. How do vehicle battles work? When the vehicle is lost, are the
> passengers and drivers also lost? What about Creature vehicles?

An enclosed vehicle is lost with everything in it. With open vehicles and
creature vehicle, characters can 'jump off' before the thing explodes or
goes down. 

> 2. Are French SW:CCG cards available yet? Can they be purchased in the
> United States?

Now you mention this. Currently they are working on this. As a matter of
fact I saw the blueprints of the cards. They were sent to our Belgium
tournament team. We had to look the translations over. However there are
quite a couple of problems with them. The translation bureau did q good
job in translating all the cards however things are not that simple.
Obviously the translaters are not SWCCG players. Some gamemechanics are
very hard to translate. For example 'present', 'present at', 'presence',
'here', 'at', 'on', ... and so on have a much more meanings then their
normal meaning. If you translate this you got to find the right words and
be consistent about it on all the cards. So, there was quite some work to
do. One of our team (squadronmember Geert Van Slambrouck) took the job
home. I don't know if he already finished.
I know he reads the digests, so if there are people out there who want to
know more about this subject and wants to have some examples and stuff,
Ask him.

> 3. Can vehicles go into interior sites? Or does it depend on the vehicle?
> How about a Tauntaun on the Death Star? 

no vehicles cannot go interior. Exceptions are the docking bays who are
both interior and exterior.

> 4. Why doesn't the Hoth 2 player game Rule book tell about creatures?
> Weren't they introduced in ANH? Is there a definitive Rule book that
> covers everything up through Dagobah? (I don't mean the FAQ)

They are not mentioned because there are no creatures in the hoth 2 player
game. 

bye,
maarten

swccg-discussion: Player's GuideI am re-posting this as I got little response the last time (from the
people who had responded before.) I suspect that because I posted it on
the weekend. Sorry to those who don't want this.

This go around, I'm sporting a new subject line...and a new catagory.
This time it's Dark Side Locations (all of 'em). As usual, ratings go
from ***** to *, with ***** being the best. Locations are different than
other cards, however. I suggest that with locations you start with ***,
and if you think a location is really good, give it another star or two,
and if you think the location sucks in a deck on that planet, take away a
star or two. Needless to say, most locations will end up with three
stars. Here's the list, and as usual, reply to me only. The results of
the DS Characters will be posted next week, and if you missed getting
your ratings in for that, you can always rate them.

Wed
1

From: Martin Arlt
Subject: Re: storm trooper deck
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000108082455/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/22747.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: storm trooper deck>There are no cometitive decks using stormtroopers, only fun ones. As

>far as playability goes stormtroopers are the cream of crap, and

>believe me I have designed many different trooper decks. They were

>actually better before Dagobah. Raithal doesn't help as much as you

>think, you have to split your forces between air and ground and if

>the opponet can just occupy Raithal your bonuses disapear. The best

>way to get rid of those annoying destinies is to use takeel to switch

>destinies. There are many ways for the LS to maneuver around takeel,

>but it helps. Grimtaash will eat the heart out of a trooper deck.

>Previous to Dagobah Grimtaash could be nullified by senses, but with

>all the anti-sense cards that have come out that's not as reliable.

>If the opponet plays an insert deck or uses a few surprise assaults

>stormtrooper decks are 100% dead. The best trooper decks aren't

>really trooper decks, they're insert decks. Trooper guards or other

>3 destiny troopers + Raithal will bring a total of fifteen destiny.

>

>____

>Joe

>[address removed]

Wow. You're sounding a bit negative there. I have to disagree with you on

most of your points, I'm afraid. I will agree that Stormtroopers aren't as

effective as they should be and that it's hard to build a tournament

winning deck around them. Still, I have built (and previously posted) a DS

trooper deck that has done far better than I ever expected. I call it

"Vader and His Lackeys." It consists of Stormtroops, D* troops, a couple

guards, and a handful of mains (Vader, Tarkin, Tagge, and Veers). It makes

use of interrupts like Dark Jedi Presence and Takeel to help out in

battles. It has no inserts or other "trick" cards. Rather, it is a

straightforward battle deck. It's not easy to play, and it doesn't win by

40, but in the hands of two players it has won 5 out of 6 games against 5

different opponents. I made it to be fun, but it surprised me by winning!

I should mention that these were non-tournament games, although most of the

players were tournament players (and one squad member who is now a Decipher

employee). 

Just remember: in this game, nothing is impossible. No cards are

completely useless. Well, maybe Tatooine: Bluffs :) And with a little

luck, there will be some good trooper-enhancing cards in CC that will make

them a little more appealing to use.

Just my $0.02

Martin....................

Martin Arlt

St. Louis, MO

From: mark-
Subject: Re: Out of Commission & personas
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000109070732/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/24192.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Out of Commission & personas> 
> I think the question is something that's been bothering me too.
> 
> Losing a card from your deck or pile is just "losing a card". So losing
> Beru to a force drain doesn't add to Luke's power, you can't use Evader
> to prevent Vader being lost from your reserve deck, etc.
> 
> So if cards in your deck or lost pile are "just a card, not a persona",
> how can Out of Commission place the persona out of play? Shouldn't the
> out of play/persona only apply to cards that are IN PLAY in the first
> place?

The distinction is kind of fuzzy, but cards like The Empire's Back 
don't care how a card got there. It's not a persona if you lose it 
as a Force Drain. However, when you're told to look and see if it's 
there, then it allows you to bring it back. 

Out Of Commision allows you to place a card out of play, but a 
persona? Was this ruled this way?

The difference here though is that these are special exceptions which 
are created by the fact that the weapon SPECIFICALLY names the 
character it is to be deployed on.

swccg-discussion: Re: Garfield's patent> > Well, I read a bit about it on Wizards homepage. Apparently they plan on
> > collecting royalties on CCG sales. I don't know how much or what games it
> > will affect ... they didn't say. 
> > 
> > Robert Irons
> > [address removed]
> 
> HA! Not a chance.
> 
> Taking somebody else's creation and changing it for your own business
> purposes is perfectly legal.
> 
> The critical cases on this, believe it or not, were about T-shirt makers
> who would make T-shirts with other people's/company's images on them.
> 
> Courts have ruled that so long as you use the image in an original
> fashion, you are entitled to do so. Flat-out copying is not legal. 
> Modifying an idea is totally legal. Look at Microsoft's victory over
> Apple.
> 
> Garfield can yell until he's blue in the face but he's not getting a
> dime.
> 

Something else to point out (and strictly my PERSONAL point of 
view). The expression "CCG" to Magic has always been "Collectible 
Card Game". "CCG" to Decipher has always been "Customizable Card 
Game". I'm not sure what the wording on the "patent" is but is seems 
a little unlikely that they can start patenting letters of the 
alphabet.

Creatures have to be present with their intended meals. 

> Second question: I was sorting my cards and I ran across the much-maligned
> (for good reason) MSE-6 Mouse droid. I read the game text again and I
> came up with an idea. Would it be possible to use the MSE-6 and docking
> bays to move Organa's Ceremonial Necklace from Yavin 4 to Hoth or
> Tatooine?

Sure. You deploy the Mouse to the targetted Imperial, move it to the 
site of the necklace, grab it and return. Being a character, the 
Mouse can use Docking Bay Transit like any other.

Subject: Re: questions before a tourney
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000109151007/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/24197.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: questions before a tourney> 1) Are Rogue gunners cummaltive? (Imperial gunners?)
> I.e. Vette w/ Luke, Wedge, Dack, Wes, 2 gunners yield +7 or +8 for a weapon
> destiny?
> 

Yes, provided they are different cards.

> 2) Asteroid Sanctuary:
> Situation: Xwing at Big One, Conquest is at the sector before Big One.
> There are two other asteroids out. The Conquest gets to the Xwing, starts
> a battle, xwing is lost. Then the asteroid destiny is drawn on the control
> phase, the Conquest is hit, lightside drains even though there is no
> presence.
> 

You cannot Force Drain with no presence, ever. 

> 3) Destiny adders:
> The FAQ says it's not cummalative, i.e.:
> I play: 2 - The Force Is Strong With This One 
> text: Lost Interrupt:
> If Luke and an Imperial with ability > 2 are involved in the same
> battle, you may add one battle destiny (add two destiny if Imperial
> is Vader).
> Assume Luke is fighting Darth Vader, would I get Luke's draw + 2 + 2 for 5
> or just Luke's draw + 2 for 3?

It is Luke's draw plus two additional draws. You can't get draws 
from additional TFISWTO that you play during that battle.

===== swccg-discussion message #24204 (yahoo) =====
Date: Sat Oct 18, 1997  6:26
pm
From: Wojciech Jankowski [address removed]
Subject: Re: New Current Rulings Doc.
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20010507001857/http://groups.yahoo.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/message/24204

Dave Price wrote:

> On Fri, 17 Oct 1997, Michael L. Dalton wrote:
>
> }2. Verification. If you search your deck for a card (say Probe droid
> }using Capt. Piett's text) and don't find it, your opponent gets to look
> }through your reserve deck to make sure it's not there.
> }
>
> WOW!!! So when I'm down to one Dagobah location left in the deck, and I don't
> find it, my opponent gets to peak at my entire reserve deck!! I can
> understand why his ruling was made. I've been annoyed when people try to keep
> inserts down by searching for something they know they don't have. People
> have also used this ability to count their reserve decks (its really obvious
> when they move their lips) or to try and figure out what thier destiny will be
> when the pile is low, or figure out if the card they need is in the active
> pile.
>
> I'm sure the guys at Deciher hated making this ruling. Its one that
> guaranteed to upset someone. Make the ruling and people complain about
> legitamte searches, or don't make the ruling and allow people to continue to
> abuse the search cards. And you can't say something vague like "and if you
> didn't know it was not in there" because people will just lie about it.
>
> My sympathies
> Dave
>

This ruling is insane! It basically allows the opponent to look through your
reserve deck if the search fails, which could give him a severe advantage, as
well
as slow the game down quite a bit. It is not "abusing" the search cards to use
them for other than finding a card, it is resourceful and shouldn't be punished.
And if you genuinely can't find the card, then you get doubly punished.

 Obi-Wans Game Text:
 "When present, at the start of a battle, may use
 1 Force to cause one opponent's character of
 ability = 1 (your choice) to move away (for free),
 or that character is immediately lost."

 Personally, I think it should be that the character's controller
has the choice to move it or lose it, but by the text, it seems
to be open to interpretation. I think that we should get an
official ruling on this.

>Regardless, the original message was just sent to give out the 
explanation

am
From: "Kevin E. Allen" [address removed]
Subject: Re: swccg-discussion-digest V1 #3534
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20010523020539/http://groups.yahoo.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/message/33834

Tewart wrote :
> Compare the number of tournament players in each region with the small
> number that attend the regionals.
> 
> The overwhelming majority of players do not compete in the regionals at
> all. They can't even get their foot in the door, so the luxuries
> lavished upon the fortunate few are just a slap in the face.
> 
> More regionals would lessen travel time for that overwhelming majority,
> increasing the participation in qualifiers by a large amount.

 You know, there's really know way around this. Right now most 
people may have a 4 or 5 hour drive to get to a Regional. You add 
more and cut it to 3. That won't be good enough... It's gotta be 
closer. More. When does it end? When every Squadron Member runs 
his own regional? No, wait, there's areas that don't have Squadron 
Members... Hmmm... <shrug>

 I think Decipher's done an outstanding job dealing with this. 
They've managed, somehow, to find a nice balance. By holding the 
regionals at major cons, they've given people something else to make 
the trip for. Of course, that set people off about having to pay a 
con fee to get in. I know... I've got it... Decipher could fly 
every Star Wars player somewhere, and the Worlds could have every 
Star Wars player in it... Hmm... But with that many people there, 
it'd run at least three or four days, so the school-goers couldn't 
attend. That wouldn't be fair... What if, for say five consecutive 
weeks, they flew people back and forth each Saturday, so every SWCCG 
player was there every weekend... Yea, that might work!!

 The above sarcasm brought to you by a confused wondering as to 
just what the hell would make you people happy.

 You know, I don't know for sure how far some people might be away 
from a Regional, but I'd guess the vast majority are within a 4-5 
hour drive... That is, really, not a bad thing. As for cost - 
carpool. Hell, last time I was at a convention, my bed was a couch 
in the hotel lobby. No reason to rent a room. Gas, food, con fee... 
That's it. Not all that bad.

 I guess what I'm really saying is if you really want to do it, 
you'll find a way to do it. If not, you'll just bitch and whine 
about how Decipher owes you something else. But that'd never happen 
here, would it?

- Phoenix
[address removed]

On Sat, 31 Jan 1998, Eric Berger wrote:

 Yes, folks, it's the battle of the cards with the longest names. Poor  humor (that's humour for the non-Americans) aside, bear with me on the  following rules question: 
 It's my turn. 

 I play Scanning Crew on my opponent. He replies with WAYTTPOU, snaring my Scanning Crew. I reply with IJASKAW. 
 Assuming there were no senses played in between, what happens next with  WAYTTPOU? 
 -a- Does WAYTTPOU automatically grab whatever next interrupt (by either  LS or DS) is played? 

nope.

 -b- Does WAYTTPOU automatically grab whatever interrupt is played during  my opponent's next turn [IJASKAW says "On Opponent's next turn, that  card must be deployed (if possible) to same location (for free).] If so,  what happens if no interrupts are played during my opponent's next turn? 

This is is, the first interrupt that is played during your opponents next turn.  Wether it is yours, or your opponents.

 -c- Does WAYTTPOU automatically grab the next Scanning Crew I play,  regardless of when I play it? 

nope.

 -d- Another possibility I was a bit too thick to consider? 

nope.

 MarkT? TomL? WarrenH? Mike Smith? Cartman? Juz? kidding... 
 -- Eric Berger 

 (Another quick question...If a destiny draw for a sense fails -- i.e. it  is higher than the highest ability char. on table -- then is the sense  card lost?) 

Nope.

Hope that Helps

===== swccg-discussion message #33856 (egroups) =====
Date: 4:33 AM

Sun
1
Feb 98
From: Magnus
Subject: IJASKAW vs. WAYTTPOU
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000229035031/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/33856.html?

swccg-discussion: IJASKAW vs. WAYTTPOU>It's my turn.
>I play Scanning Crew on my opponent.
>He replies with WAYTTPOU, snaring my Scanning Crew.
>I reply with IJASKAW.
>
>Assuming there were no senses played in between, what happens next with 
>WAYTTPOU?
>
>- -a- Does WAYTTPOU automatically grab whatever next interrupt (by either 
>LS or DS) is played?
>
>- -b- Does WAYTTPOU automatically grab whatever interrupt is played during

>my opponent's next turn [IJASKAW says "On Opponent's next turn, that 
>card must be deployed (if possible) to same location (for free).] If so, 
>what happens if no interrupts are played during my opponent's next turn?
>
>- -c- Does WAYTTPOU automatically grab the next Scanning Crew I play, 
>regardless of when I play it?
>
>- -d- Another possibility I was a bit too thick to consider?

The answer is b. If no interrupts are played during your opponent's next
turn it is not possible to deploy that card, so the opponent is free to use
WAYTTPOU in consecutive turns as normal.

>(Another quick question...If a destiny draw for a sense fails -- i.e. it 
>is higher than the highest ability char. on table -- then is the sense 
>card lost?)

No, it is used.

swccg-discussion: Re: CC/Clouds deck (long)Hi,

Saw your deck on the listserv. It looks ok, but you don't seem to have enough

power in the clouds. A dark side deck with a few ties and Tallon Rolls would

certainly render your deck helpless, as well as a heavy Cloud Car dark side

deck. Also, it seems you may be a little weak on the ground. You have plenty

of characters, but it seems if the opponent gets out Vader/Tarkin/Evazan you

would be helpless. Even just Vader would eventually out-attrition you to

death. I am working on a clouds deck myself, so this is the main reason I'd

like to discuss an effective one with you. Mine is ok right now, but it needs

some major tweaking and refining. Suggestions for cards for your deck would be

possibly one main character such as Obi or Son of would help. Although I like

your submersive tactics and blaster proficiencies. This will help in taking

out the main characters. Just some of my rambling thoughs. I hope this helps.

swccg-discussion: Dark forces CI's(sort of OT)Tell me what you think about these CI's from Dark Forces.

*General Mohc
Character Imperial Destiny 1

Deploy:5 Forfeit:6
Lore: A highly respected General. LEADER of the Dark trooper project
after the Death Star was destroyed. Personally volunteered to test Phase
3 Dark Trooper suit.

Pict: A Phase 3 Dark trooper( Helmet on probably, His head's kinda
pixelized)

Power:5 Ability:3 Armor:5 

Warrior
GT: Power +1 if at any Executor or Arc Hammer site. May fly(landspeed=2).
Once per turn may use one force and search reserve deck for a dark
trooper and deploy for free. May draw one battle destiny if not able to
otherwise. Dark trooper weapons may deploy here.

***Phase 2 Dark trooper 
Character-Imperial/droid Destiny 2

Deploy:4 Forfeit:4
Lore: A phase two dark trooper. Designed to wipe out the rebels after the
Death Star was destroyed. Equipped with cybernetic matrix and Phrik
armor. First tested on the Rebel base of Talay.

Pict: A phase 2 dark trooper(duh!)
Warrior
Power: 4 Armor:4

GT: Deploy -2 if Arc Hammer on table. (-3 if at related planet site) May
fly. (Landspeed=2) May battle and be battled. 

Phase 1 Dark trooper
Character-Imperial/droid Destiny:3
Deploy:3 Forfeit: 2
Lore: First phase of dark trooper construction. Not used widely due to
exposed endoskeleton and circuitry. Armed with metal saber to defend
minor imperial installations.

Pict: A phase 1 dark trooper

Warrior
Power:3 Armor:2

GT: Dark trooper weapons may not deploy here. Power +1 if at same site a
scomp link. May be replaced (for free)by any phase 2 dark trooper(goes to
used pile) May battle and be battled.

*Kyle Katarn
Character-Rebel Destiny:1
Deploy:4 Forfeit:5
Lore: Mercenary SPY who once worked for empire. Became a hero after
stealing Death Star plans from empire. Presently helping Rebels to
uncover clues about the Dark trooper project. 

Pilot, warrior
Power:4 Ability: 3 Force attuned

GT: Adds 2 to power of any starship he pilots(adds 3 to[I have no idea
what his ship's name is]) Power +1 at same site as Jan Ors. Once per
Battle, may use 2 force to search either reserve deck for one weapon or
device and deploy at normal use of force on him.

*Arc Hammer
Capital: Mobile Construction facility Destiny: 2

Deploy: 4 Forfeit: 6

Lore: Special starship created shortly after Battle of Yavin. Designed by
General Mohc. Built as a construction site for dark troopers. Has launch
bays for quick deployment of Dark troopers.

Power: 4 Armor: 5 Hyperspeed:3
[P] [A] SL

GT: May add 3 pilots and 6 passengers 2 TIES and/or Starfighters. Has
ship docking capability. Dark troopers deploy free here and at related
sites. If Mohc aboard, subtracts 1 from each of opponents battle
destinies at same system. Permanent pilot has ability of 2.

Dark trooper assault rifle
dark trooper weapon Destiny:2
Lore: Prototype of assault weapon for the Dark trooper project. Controls
were too large for human use. General Mohc boasted that it took out a
Rebel Base in seconds.

Pict: Dark trooper weapon stolen by Kyle on Talay

GT: Use 2 force(free on any phase 3) to deploy only to a dark trooper.
May target up to 3 characters for free. Draw two destiny. 
If destiny +2 > total ability of targets the are hit and are forfeit=0. 

DS:***Assault Cannon
Character weapon Destiny:1
Lore: Improvement of the previous assault rifle designed for a dark
trooper. Now capable of human use. Fires multiple energy pulses as well
as rockets.

Pict: the assault cannon

GT: Use 3 force to deploy on a warrior(free on Dark trooper) May target X
characters using X force (free if Mohc firing). Draw X destiny. Subtract
2 from each destiny. If total destiny > X, targets are immediately lost.

LS:**Defection
Immediate effect Destiny:2

Lore: During the time of the rebellion, many imperials felt it necessary
to defect in order to escape the treachery of the emperor. Such defectors
like Crix Madine and Leia Organa used their links with Empire to spy for
the Rebels.

Pict: Probably Crix Madine or something.

GT: Deploy on any imperial forfeit < 4, just lost or forfeited. Instead
being lost, imperial gains SPY skill and becomes your undercover spy. If
target's cover is broken by opponent, lose 3 force (lose +5 if captured
afterwards)

I haven't seen Jedi Knight yet so I don't have any CI's from there yet.
So, what do you think?

swccg-discussion: Clouds (Was Re:CC/Clouds deck)Hello again,

Well, I played that CC/Clouds deck against the same 3,720 to 1/No

mains and came out with two victories! (+1, +20) Use of Kebyc,

Lobot, and a Tibanna Gas Miner all worked in my favor for heavy force

drains and decisive battles (involving CC Troopers, of course). Soon

enough, though, it should be going up against a decent Hoth Clouds

deck that might give it a solid kick.

So what are my opinions now about Clouds? Not that anybody asked or

anything, but I like them as a deck focus. There's a large drain

capability with the Gas Miners (enhanced by Kebyc for the LS), an

advantage to using Cloud Cars rather than starfighters, and some cards

like Atmospheric Assault that add to the fun.

Thanks for your time,

Captain Paul Piett

From: "Amul B. Shah" [address removed]
Subject: Post CC-era
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20010508195832/http://groups.yahoo.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/message/33881

I was reading this post and I thought only 1 word "Odds". I was wondering
how often people come into contact with the beloved inserts? Especially
at tourney time. Mind you, there are 2 types off odds decks:
1) Sits around w/useless high destiny characters that are hidden away
safely (avg destiny=14)
2) Confrontation oriented. Often deploys and loses mains so that they do
not remain on table. Uses many destiny 3 characters (avg destiny 9).
#1 I'm not too fond of, but can be beaten if you hunt down those pesky
high destiny characters.
#2 is what I'm interested in. How often do you see these?
What do you do to prevent loss to odds?

For my dag training deck I was thinking of using only SoS+Yoda+R2D2
(destiny total 7) to prevent getting hammered by the odds.
Is the true test for a deck whether it can stand up against an Odds deck?
Thanx
A. Shah

===== swccg-discussion message #35937 (egroups) =====
Date: 1:30 AM

Sun
1
Mar 98
From: Kevin Tewart
Subject: The Forfeit Question, once and for all
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000111045557/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/35937.html?

swccg-discussion: The Forfeit Question, once and for allIt has been suggested that the modifiable & non-modifiable forfeits be

clarified, in the same manner as the cannot move and cannot-be-moved.

I think what's needed is a clarification of the forfeit rules.

We know these to be true:

#1: Vader uses his Lightsaber on Obi-Wan. Danz Borin captures Obi-Wan

w/WHAP. Danz goes to retrieve a Bounty. DS retrieves ZERO cuz Obi's

forfeit is zero.

#2: Vader uses his Lightsaber on Obi-Wan. Boba Fett (CC) captures

Obi-Wan w/WHAP. Fett goes to retrieve a Bounty. DS retrieves THREE cuz

Obi's forfeit of zero is +5, but Bounty retrieves that 5 minus another

2.

Right?

Okay. Now the hypotheticals.

A. Vader hits Obi-Wan, who is wearing a Tatooine Utility Belt. Danz

Borin captures him w/WHAP and goes to retrieve Vader's Bounty at the

Dungeon. Does DS retrieve zero or two? This revolves on whether

Vader's hit reduced only that "9" in the corner of Obi's card, or the

whole shebang.

B. At the Death Star Control Room, Vader hits Obi-Wan, who isn't wearing

anything (not a pleasant thought). Danz Borin captures him w/WHAP and

goes to retrieve Vader's Bounty at the DBC. A Rebel Commander is at the

DBC. Does the DS retrieve zero or one? (Depends on if Vader's swipe

reduced Obi's forfeit to zero forever, or if the subsequent addition of

the Rebel Commander still adds one to Obi's forfeit).

C. Luke Skywalker is fighting Admiral Motti. Luke hits Motti w/Anakin's

Lightsaber, reducing his forfeit to zero. The DS controls the Eriadu

system, which increases Motti's forfeit by +2. Can the DS still forfeit

Motti for 2 in this battle? What if Motti is also wearing a

Stormtrooper Utility Belt, is that +1 still "forfeit-able"?

And please, please, pleeeeeeeeeeeeeeeeez if you reply, point out where

in the rules/documents you are getting the source of your opinion.

--

-Kevin Tewart

"I'll put a bucket on my head and pretend to be the ancient Vorlon god,

Boojie." - Marcus Cole

===== swccg-discussion message #35938 (egroups) =====
Date: 2:04 AM

Sun
1
Mar 98
From: Chris Klint
Subject: Juneau Standard-Swiss Tourney Report (question at
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000111054252/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/35938.html?

swccg-discussion: Juneau Standard-Swiss Tourney Report (question atThis is my report on what happened at the local tourney earlier today.

Although I campaigned for a CC Scenario tourney, Dave Estes (the local TD)

thought it would be unfair to people who didn't buy into CC as much as

others, i.e, "I have Captain Han, Princess Leia and Lando to battle your

Commander Desanne and Trooper Blendin with. Who's gonna win?" As a

result, we played a standard-issue sanctioned-Swiss tournament.

After some consideration, I built a Hoth turtleshell-type Clouds deck,

viciously impossible to counter. (I still can't believe it when I see Echo

Base become a series of drain-1 sites...ick.) For the Dark Side, I stuck

with my standard Garrison/Trooper deck, with the addition of two Ability,

Ability, Abilitys as an experiment. Here's how it went:

Game 1: I was randomly paired as Dark with Lake, one of the local Jawas

(you know, those little kids with endless pockets matched only by their

lack of experience.) It was the poor guy's first tourney game; he started

with a Throne Room and put a pilot there, but got kicked off Hoth by six

Snowtroopers and Trooper Assaults. Horrific battle losses from the

troopers Elising about Hoth made it a quick and big win, some 36

differential.

Game 2: This was my Hoth Clouds against Adam Garrison, a local kid known

for a decent quantity of skill in the game and huge quantities of cards he

cares little for. (How little, you ask? He sold me two each All Too Easys

and I Am Your Fathers for $2.50...) An early attempt to control the Hoth

system was thwarted by Luke/Red 5/Artoo, Wedge/Red 2 and Tantive IV. After

the destruction of several Star Destroyers, the fighting moved to the North

Ridge where their crews had shuttled down. Combat was so thick there that

Adam fully expended two Septoid Droids' forfeit on the front lines.

Obi-Wan, KFC, Electrobinoculars and a Bacta Tank were holding off a force

which included Darth Vader, Danz Borin in a Cloud Car, IG-88 and Admiral

Ozzel, among others! Amazingly, 37 cards remained in my Force at the end

of hostilities.

Game 3: Tim Shockley, a strong player, had a potent Dagobah Clouds strategy

for his Light deck. One problem sunk Tim, though: he elected to use Cloud

Cars for sector control, and even an Encamped Bog Clearing wouldn't allow

their deployment. After a heated argument, he settled for using Great

Warrior to shut down my Garrison deck and trying to patch up my remaining

Force drains with Undercover spies which promptly got Trampled. Despite

Mosep draining 3 sites from Reserve, a continuously active Imperial Decree

keeping his only point of Force drain (Anakin's Lightsaber) inactive, and

the adverse effects of Ability3 on his character-thin deck, something like

five On The Edges kept him around for a timed 8-card win.

***This is the question mentioned in the title***

I must digress here, however, to ask an important question. Tim maintains

that he should have been able to deploy Cloud Cars on Dagobah, but there's

no way I know of that the Cars could have been deployed there. All the

Dag/Clouds decks I see here focus on various types of starfighters

launching from the Bog Clearing. AFAIK, the *only* way Light could get

Cars on Dagobah would be to ferry them in from elsewhere on Medium

Transports. Can anybody confirm this?

***This is the question mentioned in the title***

Game 4: I used my Light deck again, against Tim Powers, local SW:CCG's

"other" Tim. He was playing an efficient Death Squadron/Asteroid deck I

contributed cards and tactics to, a mixed blessing given the brutal record

of my Asteroid deck in tourney play. I'd clearly forgotten how effective

those tactics were; my "lucky" first-turn Luke/Saber/Prof was immediately

Overloaded with a 0 draw! The game was a close fight throughout, involving

advanced (by Juneau standards) strategy. I had to send in Wedge and Biggs

in their ships to retake Yavin 4 from Tim, who was using it in conjunction

with Hoth to activate an Imperial Decree. He's the only other player in

town who uses Decree-nobody else has yet grasped its usefulness, and even

he hadn't really thought about it when I first mentioned it. I eventually

won, but only by 8 Force.

Game 5: A *very* strange Dark game against Adam, and a perfect illustration

of overreliance on a single card. This was a "crazy" deck he'd mentioned

the presence of 30 Corvettes in, so I was worried about my deck's lack of a

space arm. As it turned out, he'd been blowing smoke to disguise his

deck's true intent: using Leia Of Alderaan and troopers to dominate Hoth

and specifically defeat my Garrison deck. After several turns, I had

Veers/Blizzard 1 and most of my Snowtroopers at the Mountains and ready to

move "inland." I kicked out an Ability3 and drew some Force, adding cards

to my hand to create a well-rounded suite of red cards like Trooper

Assaults and Elises. He kicks out Leia Organa to the Mountains, using her

spy ability, adds Leia Of Alderaan and throws down enough Rebels to

immediately cancel my Ability3. That's when Leia got Trampled. Things

went downhill for Adam from there, due to sheer forgetfulness. (Later,

more troopers showed up two sites away from a Princess Leia with LOA, and

were ground into meat because Adam was counting on LOA's "same and

*adjacent*" effects. Heh heh heh.) I won with about 30 Force left.

Game 6: The final and decisive game of the tourney, versus Tim Shockley,

still one point ahead of me from the bitter timed win of Game 3. Death

Squadron quickly took the Asteroids, but I got out Hoth, a Cloud and a

Defensive Perimeter for FDs of 4 on the planet. Steady drainage continued

on both sides, wearing both him and me down by attrition. At a crucial

moment, 1) I finally deployed and manned two more Clouds, 2) Tim drew 15 or

20 cards from his deck but left only 4 Force, forgetting about my Kebyc,

and 3) I used On The Edges to retrieve 9 cards. I only won by 11 Force.

I got an R2-D2 T-shirt, apporpriate considering the vital role he played in

my deck. (The main force in the deck was a full set of Red Squadron

X-wings, notoriously lacking in astromechs. To correct this problem, do

some fun things when Dark drew destiny and just on general principles, I

used two Artoos in my Clouds deck. He saw use as Luke's 'mech, bailed

Biggs out of certain doom, blocked Force drains as an Undercover spy, even

got used as forfeit bait in conjunction with a Crash Site Memorial!)

Additionally, I scored three packs of WB Premiere from the first of the

enhanced prize-support packages we've gotten here.

Oh, yes, I almost forgot: this was an extraordinary tournament for freaky

events. Here's just some of what I saw when I made a round of the games in

progress after my last game:

* Adam was playing Dark against Lenard Smiley, a big guy with yet another

Dag/Clouds design. Adam had managed to "import" to the Clouds, all the way

from Kashyyyk, Obsidian 7/8, a TIE Avenger with Danz Borin and a Boosted

TIE Cannon, as well as CC Fett in Slave I! Lenard only controlled one

sector, and that one only because he'd retreated all of his fighters there.

Lenard had this sector passing on its FD+1 to an Encamped Princess Leia,

with Anakin's Saber and Saber Prof, plus a Presence Of The Force Lenard had

IHABFATed from elsewhere, at the *Training Area.* As if that wasn't stupid

enough, Adam was taking the drain each turn...

* I went over to watch Matt Monagle playing his Dark deck. A battle was

concluding: eight Rebels, including several mains and Tatooine Utility

Belts, had just lost by 10 in the Light Cantina to a stack of five

nondescript characters. Surprised, I looked them over: Vader, his

Lightsaber, Dr. Evazan, a Rodian, Djas Puhr and Dannik Jerriko. I asked

Matt, "What did you do-play I Have You Now or Levitation Attack?" He

smiles, grabs an ominously large pile of Light cards near his Lost Pile and

hands them to me. I look at 'em: Obi-Wan, Momaw Nadon, Lando, Jek and

Dutch. Light forfeits a Luke; Matt spends 1, grabs him and adds him to the

stack with a slurping noise. He looks at me: "Soup-M'mm M'mm Good!" I

quickly walk away, hoping I *never* have to face that nightmare in a

game...

Anyhow, if you're still awake, thanks for reading! I'll be adding

summaries of the decks involved for review shortly after this message.

TTYL!

===== swccg-discussion message #35939 (egroups) =====
Date: 2:06 AM

Sun
1
Mar 98
From: Chris Klint
Subject: My decks from the Juneau tourney
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000111062804/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/35939.html?

swccg-discussion: My decks from the Juneau tourneyHere's a bit of something you can waste your time on: any suggestions for

either of these decks? I'd like to hear 'em!

Dark Side: Garrison/Trooper Assault Deck

Characters (20)

Admiral Ozzel (deploy, Veers power bonus)

Danz Borin (elite AT-AT pilot)

Darth Vader (excellent ground asset, best TTMG pilot)

Zuckuss (ability, replaces a second Vader)

DS-61-2 (elite AT-AT pilot)

DS-61-3 (power, elite AT-AT pilot)

DS-61-4 (elite AT-AT pilot)

General Veers x2 (deck theme)

Lieutenant Cabbel (AT-AT pilot, leader bonus)

Mosep (Garrison drains from Reserve)

Snowtrooper x6 (Echo Base invasion force)

Snowtrooper Officer x3 (wicked with troopers)

Effects (6)

Imperial Decree x2 (Why not? >:))

Reactor Terminal (protection against overdraw)

There Is No Try (protect red cards)

Ability, Ability, Ability x 2 (take advantage of numerous characters)

Epic Events (2)

Target The Main Generator x2 (support deck objectives)

Interrupts (15)

Boring Conversation Anyway x2 (anti-Mains N' Toys card, see analysis)

Elis Helrot x2 (Snowtrooper backup)

Stunning Leader x2 (Snowtrooper backup)

Tactical Support (Snowtrooper backup)

Trample x2 (crush the Rebellion!)

Trooper Assault x3 (Snowtrooper backup)

Walker Garrison x5 (deck theme)

Locations (8)

Hoth: Defensive Perimeter x2 (Force generation, biggest drain on Hoth)

Hoth: Echo Corridor

Hoth: Echo Docking Bay

Hoth: Ice Plains x2 (Force generation)

Hoth: Mountains (starting location; cheap deploy)

Dagobah: Cave (Force generation, anti-Yoda, possibly FATCs)

Vehicles (5)

Blizzard 1

Blizzard 2

Blizzard Walker x3

Weapons (2)

AT-AT Cannon x2 (TTMG)

Basically, I let the first eight cards determine initial strategy.

Sometimes you get an Officer, a few Snowtroopers and related red cards;

sometimes you get a combination of walkers and pilots. Deploy accordingly.

Troopers are quick and best suited to unshielded scenarios where they have

the flexibility of Elis; walkers are slower but have more staying power and

support Garrison drainage. Because your forces are weakest inside Echo

Base (whether or not they're using it) opponents tend to fight you there

rather than outside. That's where Stunning Leader comes in. In addition

to its obvious trooper-support uses, SL works with Veers, Vader or Zuckuss

like a Houjix, only it doesn't require their forfeiture. Next turn you can

move your man away, or better yet Elis in some reinforcements and battle...

Current weaknesses include a lack of Monnoks and Failures At The Caves,

plus a susceptibility to Mains N' Toys destiny adders. Borings are a start

against them, but things I'd like to do (a few WHAPs to seize mains, or

There'll Be Hell To Pays to shut down all-too-common On The Edge retrieval)

I can't find space for in this deck. I'd appreciate ideas on how to

reconfigure the deck.

Light Side: Hoth Aerial Assault Deck:

Characters (14)

Biggs Darklighter (matches Red 3)

Jek Porkins (matches Red 6)

Kal'Falnl C'ndros (forfeit, Tanking)

Kebyc (Clouds drain from Reserve)

Luke Skywalker x2 (good ground character, matches Red 5)

Obi-Wan Kenobi x2 (main ground asset on Hoth)

R2-D2 x2 (spy, astromech, destiny)

R2-X2 (fills Red Squadron 'mech slots)

Tibanna Gas Miner x2 (Cloud drainage)

Wedge Antilles (matches Red 2)

Devices (2)

Electrobinoculars x2 (destiny-rigging)

Effects (8)

Bacta Tank (regen characters esp. KFC)

Crash Site Memorial (can save over 20% of deck cards)

Echo Base Operations (secondary deck objective)

Lightsaber Proficiency x2 (it's like carrying a second lightsaber!)

Traffic Control x2 (prevent overdraw, cycle high-destiny cards)

Undercover (yet another fun use for Artoo!)

Interrupts (7)

Gift Of The Mentor x2 (get lightsabers, high destiny, rarely played for BD)

Nabrun Leids x2 (off-planet mobility)

On The Edge x3 (limited retrieval options, mainly using 5s)

Locations (14)

Hoth: Defensive Perimeter (2-point drain under the shield)

Hoth: Echo Command Center (Force, Echo site for EBO)

Hoth: Echo Corridor (Echo site)

Hoth: Echo Docking Bay (starfighter storage, Echo site)

Hoth: Main Power Generators (starting location)

Hoth: North Ridge (outermost marker)

Clouds x4 (deck theme)

Hoth x2 (Clouds deployment)

Dantooine (Force, possible EBO system)

Clak'dor VII (Force, possible EBO system)

Starships (5)

Red Leader in Red 1 (excellent all-in-one ship)

Red 2 (cloud/space control)

Red 3 (cloud/space control)

Red 5 (best ship in the deck with Luke and Artoo)

Red 6 (cloud/space control, destiny)

Redemption (Tanking, pilot transfers)

Tantive IV (power, excellent pilot platform)

Vehicles (4)

Incom T-16 Skyhopper x3 (Harpooning, free shuttling through Clouds)

Luke's T-16 Skyhopper (pilot slot, Harpooning, free shuttling through Clouds)

Weapons (4)

Anakin's Lightsaber (Force drain, makes Luke a credible combat threat)

Obi-Wan's Lightsaber (Force drain, Saber Prof)

Power Harpoon x2 (Skyhoppers take down walkers!)

Strategy with this one is simple: Draw until you have the cards to assemble

an impenetrable drainage bastion, then use a Traffic Control to send back

everything you don't need and On The Edge until Dark curls up and dies.

Depending on the circumstances, you can kick out an EBO and hit Dark for 7

more each turn with the deck's three systems, two of which the opponent

can't even deploy to. After you set up what you need, most if not all of

your Force should be consumed by your Binos, for OTE as well as assuring

superior battle destiny.

Red Squadron ensures destiny draws, but most of them are limited by their

lack of astromechs. At least you aren't very susceptible to Astromech

Shortage. :) The three in the deck attempt to compensate for this, but if

you're deploying to Dantooine or Clak'dor, the ship's usually there to

stay. Of course you can evacuate pilots with Redemption and the T4, but

it's rather awkward. Due to the astromech situation, I've given canning

EBO some thought, but I'm torn between the need to fit in some 'mechs and

Grimtaashes, and the fact that EBO and the two systems could be draining

for half of what the whole 15 or 20-card Clouds system averages in play.

I'd like to hear your verdict on EBO, as well as what you think of its

shortcomings.

That's what I'm playing with right now. Thanx for your time!

===== swccg-discussion message #36956 (egroups) =====
Date: 3:08 AM

Wed
1
Apr 98
From: Alan Ball
Subject: Re: Shocking Info-Sense
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000110000351/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/36956.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Shocking Info-SenseMichael L. Dalton wrote:

> On Tue, 31 Mar 1998, Alan Ball wrote:
> > Wait a minute. I asked this EXACT question 3 months ago and was told
> > yes, you can (provided SI was played first due to the timing rules). SI
> > would resolve, leaving SC as the top Interrupt of the stack and would now
> > be eligible to be Sensed. What happened?
>
> >From my perspective it's like this:
> Dark: play Scanning Crew
> Light: no response (time to sense is now)
> Dark: no response
> (Scanning Crew resolves)
> Light: play Shocking Info
> Dark: no response
> Light: no response
> (Shocking Info resolves)

<snip>

Sorry, that's wrong. Read SI again. It says, "If opponent IS ABOUT TO
scan...." (capitalization used to reference the important part of GT). That
means your opponent has not yet scanned your hand. If he has not yet scanned
your hand, SC could not have already resolved (it is still on the table
awaiting a valid response). You cannot play SI after SC has already looked
through your hand. It must be played before. Therefore, SI and Sense should
be valid (and has been previously ruled by Tom as being valid).
