swccg-discussion: Re: swccg-discussion-digest V1 #3760On Wed, 1 Apr 1998, Salion wrote:

> Just a quick question...
> 
> In tournament reports, I find people saying..
> 
> "I lost X (unique persona) in a battle but that was alright because next
> turn I deployed another one"
> 
> How can you do this? I thought it was prevented by the persona rule..

Yes, it's perfectly legal - so long as X was simply lost but not placed
out of play.

swccg-discussion: Re: swccg-discussion-digest V1 #3759I thought that it was based on World War II, because the Jedi (Jews) were
exterminated by Hitler (Emperor). Also, the Nazi warriors were called
stormtroopers.

>Zimmerman, Eric wrote:
>
>> There was a book published about 20 years ago that had a 
>theory that
>> Star Wars was loosely based on the Bible.
>
>Speculation:
>I've always thought it was loosely based on the Revolutionary war.
>You know, a fight back against impossible odds, everyone is highly 
>motivated,
>the "Empire"'s officers are arrogant (and all of them have British 
>accents), a
>"King" type character. Very loosely. I've always thought it was 
>significant that
>the Imperial Officers had British accents, just not sure precisely 
>why...
>
>Relatively sure about this stuff:
>I know for a fact that the characters of Luke and Obi-Wan are at least 
>loosely
>based on the characters in the John wayne movie "The Searchers", and 
>also, some
>of the characters in the film "Seven Samurai". Some scenes are 
>strongly
>reminiscent of scenes from "Yojimbo"(particularly the Cantina fight). 
>I don't
>think Lucas had a particularly christian bent in mind when he was 
>writing it, he
>
>was influenced by the writings of Huston Smith, I believe, who wrote a 
>textbook
>of the world's religions. It was mainly an effort to replace the lost 
>mythology
>of Western movies in American culture. That's how I've always 
>understood it, and
>
>Lucas seemed to imply the same in the introductory film to the Star 
>Wars: The
>Magic of Myth exhibit at the National Air and Space Museum.
>
>
>
>
>- --
>- ----------------------------

>list of
>all the people I've murdered--by letting you live.
>
>The Dark Knight Returns -- Frank Miller

>As I understand it, regular sealed deck tournaments, where you buy one
>or two starters and some packs from one or more expansions, are still
>legal.
>
>I'd say you couldn't allow sealed decks where you bring your 18 new
>cards, because some players won't have them yet. Such a format would
>exclude anyone who had not yet purchased the Sealed Deck product.
>
>Also, I would guess that Decipher will release future Official Sealed
>Deck products that contain different boosters and maybe more
>not-available-elsewhere cards.
>

>I completely agree. I had these cards introduced to me by a player on
>CardTable; the introduction wasn't pleasent on my part. Then I was 
>told to
>add one or two to my two tourney decks I posted a while back. Man, 
>they
>rock. I'm really surprised not that many ppl used them. They've 
>become
>just as big a staple in my decks as Houjix/Ghhhk and Grimnock are. 
>Plus,
>You Are Beaten is great for canceling the ever annoying Uncontrollable
>Fury. There's almost always a use for them b/c we all know taking one
>person out of a battle will win games. Vader/Tarkin isn't safe 
>anymore.
>Neither is Han/Chewie, Han/Luke and so on. You have to make sure you 
>have
>more than 2 ppl protecting a site (more than 3 if you play against 
>someone
>like Jesse here who will play two on you). To recap, I give You Are
>Clashed (or maybe Clash of Beatens?) two big thumbs up.

>Date: Tue, 31 Mar 1998 18:02:52 -0500 (EST)
>From: "Michael L. Dalton" [address removed]
>Subject: Re: Shocking Info-Sense
>
>On Tue, 31 Mar 1998, Alan Ball wrote:
>> Wait a minute. I asked this EXACT question 3 months ago and was 
>told
>> yes, you can (provided SI was played first due to the timing rules). 
> SI
>> would resolve, leaving SC as the top Interrupt of the stack and 
>would now
>> be eligible to be Sensed. What happened?
>
>>From my perspective it's like this:
>Dark: play Scanning Crew
> Light: no response (time to sense is now)
> Dark: no response
>(Scanning Crew resolves)
>Light: play Shocking Info
> Dark: no response
> Light: no response
>(Shocking Info resolves)
>
>or
>
>Dark: play Scanning Crew
> Light: sense
> Dark: no response
> Light: no response
> (sense resolves)
>(Scanning crew canceled)
>
>basically, the only way to Sense and Shocking Info Scanning Crew is if 
>
>the sense fails, because Shocking Info must be played after Scanning 
>Crew 
>resolves and Sense must be played before it resolves and would cancel 
>that resolution if it succeeds.
>

From: Michael L. Dalton
Subject: Re: Shocking Info-Sense
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000110130903/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/36964.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Shocking Info-SenseOn Wed, 1 Apr 1998, Alan Ball wrote:
> Michael L. Dalton wrote:
> 
> > On Tue, 31 Mar 1998, Alan Ball wrote:
> > > Wait a minute. I asked this EXACT question 3 months ago and was told
> > > yes, you can (provided SI was played first due to the timing rules). SI
> > > would resolve, leaving SC as the top Interrupt of the stack and would now
> > > be eligible to be Sensed. What happened?
> >
> > >From my perspective it's like this:
> > Dark: play Scanning Crew
> > Light: no response (time to sense is now)
> > Dark: no response
> > (Scanning Crew resolves)
> > Light: play Shocking Info
> > Dark: no response
> > Light: no response
> > (Shocking Info resolves)
> 
> <snip>
> 
> Sorry, that's wrong. Read SI again. It says, "If opponent IS ABOUT TO
> scan...." (capitalization used to reference the important part of GT). That
> means your opponent has not yet scanned your hand. If he has not yet scanned
> your hand, SC could not have already resolved (it is still on the table
> awaiting a valid response). You cannot play SI after SC has already looked
> through your hand. It must be played before. Therefore, SI and Sense should
> be valid (and has been previously ruled by Tom as being valid).

Actually, Tom's ruled a lot of things, most recently:

> Can you Sense and Shocking Info the same Scanning Crew?

You cannot.

Hope that helps!

Tom
---------------
Looking at Shocking Info again, I guess maybe you're right, I was just 
trying to explain Tom's ruling which was most certainly that you cannot 
sense and shocking info the same scanning crew. It's obviously some 
timing issue, involving what constitutes 'about to'. Is it an 
interrupt that's resolved but not taken it's action yet? maybe we could get 
Tom to explain his ruling? - Mike.

===== swccg-discussion message #36982 (yahoo) =====
Date: Wed Apr 1, 1998  8:29
pm
From: [address removed] (Curtiss Murphy)
Subject: Ties in the Dark...
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20010508200853/http://groups.yahoo.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/message/36982

Heya All, 

Well, I got tired of hearing about the old SITDD deck, so I decided to
see what else could be done with that abusive Shot in the Dark card. 
I'm happy (and perhaps lucky) to report that i have beaten SITDD 2 out 
of 3 times in sanctioned play. I'm VERY pleased with that considering 
the level of players I was competing against.

Welp. Here's a new abusive version to amuse yourselves with. Tweak at 
will, have fun, and don't forget to Tie a yellow ribbon... :)

Ties in the Dark
---------------------
Locations 12
Kessel (SL), Hoth, Tatooine, Yavin IV, Kashyyyk, Bespin, 
Anoat, Raithal, Kiffex (x2), Dantooine, Bespin:CC

Ships 20
Victory (x2), Avenger, Stalker, Devestator, Conquest, Tyrant, 
Executor, DS Assault Squad (DSAS), obsidian 7, obsidian 8
Tie Scout (x2), Tie Fighter (x5), Tie Advanced (x2)

Characters 11
Ponda Boba, Dr E, Office Mkae, Danz Borin, Imp Pilot (x2),
Imp Helmsman (x2), U3P0, R1-G4 (x2)

Interrupts/Effects 17
Shot in the Dark (x7)
Imperial Supply (x4) - (i think that's the one - gives you force...)
3,720 to 1 (x4)
Houjix (x2)

Welp. That's all she wrote. Strategies kinda interesting. On your 
first, or at the latest 2nd turn, use SITD to set up at as many 
drains in space as possible. Once you've got SITD out and 5-6 systems, 
you will have plenty of early initiative to set up MASSIVE drains 
in space. Oh, the 3,720 is just a suplemental thing to do a little
damage here and there to your opponent. 

Granted, its not as quick a kill as SITD, but its also not as risky. 
You have plenty of power to back up your systems and your drains 
can't be canceled like Dark Deal.

Well, happy Star Wars to you. Any ideas? Note that the named ships
and other rares aren't really essential (except kiffex). Replace em 
with Imp Starships/victories or Imp Pilots if thats what you got. 
Makes for another cheap (less than $25) deck to play... 

Another note, if you want to go for more power and protection in 
space, then take out the inserts, the droids, and DSAS and replace 
em with lots of powerful pilots like the DS61 boys, Motti, Ozzel, 
Evax, Cabbel, etc...

Later, 
Curtiss.

* Appropriate Technologies, Inc. (http://www.app-tech.com/staff/cmurphy)

From: Jeff Heskett
Subject: Re: Tom's Answer to "Zero as Even"
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000303141411/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/41160.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Tom's Answer to "Zero as Even"> So what do you do w/ R2D2's text when a 0 is drawn, hmmm?

> 

> Gotcha!

R2-D2: "While aboard any starfighter, adds 2 to power, maneuver and 

hyperspeed (3 on Red 5). If at a Scomp link when opponent draws 

destiny of: 1-3, you may activate one Force. 4-6, you may draw top 

card from Reserve Deck. "

Salacious Crumb: "Opponent may initiate battle at same site for free. 

At same or adjacent site, whenever an opponent draws a card for 

battle destiny, if it is: Even, opponent must use 1 Force (if 

possible) Odd, destiny card is lost. (AH-hahahahaha!) "

Perhaps the greatest flaw in human logic is the knack for using the 

outcome as the "given"s. Sort of like the out-of-state tourist who 

buys a lottery ticket and wins and assumes everyone in the state

is rich too. 

I happen to think zero is both even and neither even or odd. Like 

infinity it just kinda sits there and does whats needed of it. (If 

the need arises it could probaby be odd too--hey, it could happen! 

Just look at all the versatile uses of infinity)

swccg-discussion: Re: Scum and Villainy questionPat George wrote:

> For those of you who have the card or can see it, what is that symbol in
> the gametext that says "...aliens and <icon> starship pilots...." I'm
> pretty sure it's the independent icon but I just want to be sure. Also,
> what's an independent starship pilot? Is it any pilot that is piloting an
> independent starship? Is it any pilot who owns an independent starship (ie

>

it's any character piloting an independent starship. You can even have Vader
trained to be a smuggler piloting the Mist Hunter and you can still use their
S+V. Also, all independent starship owners are also aliens or droids so they
meet the requirements of S+V anyway.

From: "Zimmerman, Eric" [address removed]
Subject: RE: Hit vs Lost vs Immediately Lost vs Captured etc...
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20010507001828/http://groups.yahoo.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/message/41175

'Hit' refers to getting targeted by a weapon and being successful.

With Bossk Mortar Gun 'in the case you are talking about' the gun explodes
causing the shooter to be 'lost' s. They are not immediately lost but more
a casualty of the battle. Although I'm not sure what else the mortar gun
says you should be able to collect any types of forfeits from the targeter.

I hope this was helpful.

Zim

> ----------
> From: [address removed][SMTP:[address removed]]
> Sent: Friday, May 01, 1998 1:04 AM
> To: [address removed]
> Subject: Hit vs Lost vs Immediately Lost vs Captured etc...
> 
> While playing a couple of games against my brother-in-law recently, we
> ran into a little dilemma:
> 
> "Hit" is well-defined. "Immediately Lost" is pretty easy. "Captured" is
> most likely right away (like "Crashed") but we're not sure. We had NO
> idea what "Lost" on Bossk's Mortar Gun meant. Is it lost immediately
> (then why doesn't it say "immediately")? Do we wait until end of turn
> (then why isn't it "hit")? 
> 
> We came up with this: it gets to stay until the end of the battle but it
> doesn't count for forfeit.
> 
> I'll tell you in advance that neither of us thought that was even close
> to the "official" answer, but we liked it just fine for the game.
> 
> I must say that it was GREAT fun using Fett's Hidden Weapons to capture
> Yoda. Hee hee! (MMmmrrgh! Tough net this is! Useless is my Gimer
> Stick!)

swccg-discussion: Re: swccg-discussion-digest V1 #4057>In a message dated 98-05-31 04:01:01 EDT, [address removed] writes:
>
><<

> to you. They have, I have. Live with it :)
> >>
>
>Him I didn't outright call a liar, you I might as well.
>
>Premiere BB was not as random as you are suggesting.
>
>The falcon is not worth as much as a character and I seriously doubt it's as
>rare as a darth vader. the main reason it's not worth as much as a character
>is it requires a pilot. Why not just stick a corellian corvette out? or the
>Tantive 4? or a couple of x-wings? Immunity to attrition ya say? In a major
>battle with several dark side star fighters will take care of that, or even
>the hound's tooth with a little support.

Um...Kevin, have you sat down and figured out what you can *do* with the
Falcon? Captain Han is easily the sickest main I've seen because of what
he does to it, Han's Toolkit (not to mention Wedge/Corellian Slips or Rayc
Ryjerd) precludes Tallon Rolling it, Special Modifications/Light Maneuvers
is like having an Corvette bolted onto the side of the ship for fire
support, and then there's the destiny adders. Just Han and Chewie aboard
the Falcon allow four battle destiny with Life Debt, six if you can play
Punch It!; adding Luke (either as a passenger or in Red 5 with R2-D2) lets
you play Don't Get Cocky, Rug Hug and The Force Is Strong With This One.
Throw in Leia (and Threepio, if Luke's in Red 5) for a few more adders plus
playing an Artoo, I Have A Bad Feeling About This for the humiliation
value. At this point it's possible to build an all-frickin'-rare, deadly
efficient Light space deck *much* more detrimental to the game than
Plastoid Armor ever could be.

Personally, I think that the Falcon and Light's other "super-ship," Red 5,
warrant the creation of what's effectively a Hidden Weapons for Star
Destroyers. (If you've seen this one before, my apologies for reposting
this CI.)

Dark Used Interrupt *Imperial Inspection 4

"Star Destroyer captains are authorized to conduct spot inspections of
vessels in Imperial space. Fugitive Rebels, contraband weapons shipments
and illegal Kessel spice are grounds for immediate detainment."

Pic: A stock light freighter sits in the shadow of an ISD, the local sun
partially hidden behind the massive warship's hull...

During any control phase, use X Force to target a lone opponent's starship
present with one of your Star Destroyers. Any Kessel Run targeting a
smuggler aboard target is canceled. Randomly "inspect" X cards from the
opponent's hand. If any of them is a Rebel or weapon, Star Destroyer
immediately captures target. (Immune to Sense.)

The "inspection" game mechanic here is fascinating to play. Dark might use
a Scanning Crew to determine Light's "cargo" and decide not to send Obi-Wan
to the Used Pile, in hopes of finding him during a subsequent Inspection.
Whether Dark finds a Rebel or weapon depends on how much "time" (Force) it
spends on the Inspection. On the Light Side, it's possible to come clean
with the Empire and never lose ships to Inspections; all one has to do is
use only cards that don't appear on the Imperial contraband list... >:)

swccg-discussion: Jawa S&V Deck For Review. Please CommentThis is a jawa deck I've cobbled together from JP and premiere stuff I had

left over. Tell me what you think.

Charachters

Chall Bekan

Herat x3

Jawa x20

Wittin x3

Creatures

Bubo

Effects

Ability, ability ability

Den of Theives

Jawa Pack x10

S&V (starting)

Interrupts

Abyssin Ornament x4

Dune Sea Sabbac x2

Elis Helrot x2

T'wilek Advisor x3

Sites

JP audience chamber

Cantina

Jundland wastes

Lar's Moisture Farm

Tusken Canyon

Vehicles

Skiff x3

The idea is to pull Wittin First turn with the audience chamber GT. Then,

using S&V, pop down as many jawas and jawa packs a possible in as short a

time as possible to make sure Wittin doesn't die. From there on, the idea

is to cause the opponent damage through battle, and if he plays non

interactive, you can use AAA and the drains to flush him out.

Subject: Please critique my deck
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000108232859/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/45550.html?

swccg-discussion: Please critique my deckBelow is a fairly new deck I have been using.
I am still unsure of its tournement worthiness so critique away please!
(Its currently 3-0 up)

Fettster Mining Inc. (TM)

Lore:
With Jabba vanquished and the Empire scattered, the galaxy was 
now open to the wiles of the corporations.
After escaping from the Sarlacc, Boba Fett decides the mining
industry is more profitable than bounty hunting and, using
Jabbas now-empty palace as his base of operations, begins
his rise to big buisiness.

Locations (7):
Tat: Jabbas Palace (STARTING)
3 x Clouds
JP: Audience Chamber
JP: Dungeon
JP: Rancor Pit

Characters (14):
Boba Fett (CC)
Gailid
3 x Tibanna Gas Miner
Bane Malar
Djas Phur
Dengar
Lietenant Commander Ardan
Captain Khurgee
Velken Tezeri
M'iiyoom Onith 
Ugnaught
WED15-17 Septiod Droid

Starships/Vehicles (7):
Death Star Assault Squadron
Obsidian 7
Obsidian 8
4 x Cloud Car

Weapons/Devices (5)
Dark Jedi Lightsaber
Mandalorian Armor
2 x Luke Seeker
Han Seeker

Effects (1):
There Is No Try (STARTING)

Interrupts (26):
Twilek Advisor (STARTING)
4 x Scanning Crew
3 x Control
3 x Defensive Fire
3 x Voyeur
2 x A Dangerous Time
2 x Limited Resources
2 x Monnok
Ghhhk
2 x Stunning Leader
3 x Hidden Weapons

Main Strategies:
REPPRESION, CLOUD DRAIN
Sub Strategies:
SEEKERS, HIDDEN WEAPONS

Strategy:
Start with Jabbas Palace, pull the sites, this gives adequate force
(helped by oponents and clouds).
While searching for Jabbas Sites, you can see if Gailid and Boba Fett 
(the two key characters) are in the force pile. If so, grab them.
Else use Jabbas Audience chamber to deploy at least one of them.

Drains can then be set up on the ground (Jabbas Palace) and in the 
Tatoinne Clouds. The fighting chraacters represent characters
who are good on there own or with little back up. 
Use Boba Fett to protect Gailid at the Audience chamber, this
helps the ground drain.

The scanning crews, defensive fires, monnoks and voyeurs slow him
quite considerably, especially if he is playing with mains'n'toys.
(Khurgee + Miiyoom Onith help with this).

The stunning leaders and hidden weapons should more than see you through
the majority of fights (over 50% of destinies are 2 or 3, making
capturing with the hidden weapons pretty easy). Mandalorian Armour 
(almost) doubles the Hidden Weapons threat. Note that bounty hunters
(4 in the deck) can deliver captives and retrieve force without a bounty.
This is nice if the opertunity arrives but the plan should not be forced.

The remaining trick is the seekers whom, with Velken Teezri are deadly,
the Ugnaught pulls back the seekers from the grave if neccessary.

Finally the DJL can increase drains or be used offensivly with either
Djas, Bane or Fettster. The septoid droid is for forfeit fodder and
is surprisingly useful.

All comments, (especially constructive critisisms) would be highly appreciated.
Could replys be forwarded to [address removed] as well please as I get the
digest version of the list and cutting and pasting is a bit of a hassle.
Ta.

A last final note, Vader and Tarkin are in one of my other decks and are not
available. They would have been nice, I know.

______________________________________________________
Iain Reid :-)>

swccg-discussion: Re: HERE IT IS: Strategy: Controling Sense AlterAt 04:23 AM 6/1/98 -0400, [address removed] is being quoted thusly...
>I
> Here you guys go. Give me a review of my little article here. 

i don't know if it's been mentioned before to you, but please... never ever
send any attachments to the list. lots of people (myself includes, and
mark too i think) really don't appreciate files being sent to us without
warning. i'm sure it's virus free, but... 

with me it's just a pet peeve... i'm surely not going to read it anyway
(since i delete 75% of the stuff from the list), so all it's doing is
taking up my HD space.

so just a thought for the future [and this goes to everyone], if you are
planning to send an attachment of ANY kind to the list, first send a
message asking if anyone wants to see what you have, and then send it to
them PERSONALLY!

From: Mike Smith
Subject: Re: Starfighter/combet vehicle interchangeable?
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000109135823/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/45559.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Starfighter/combet vehicle interchangeable?It's possible the person asking the question doesn't understand or
know about the Hoth rule about pilots boosting certain vehicles. 
Here's the entry from the Hoth rules, under Pilots: "Characters acting
as pilots now have the capabiltiy to enhance combat vehicles and
shuttle vehicles as well as starships. For example, Darth Vader 'adds
3 to power of any starship he pilots.' Thus, this new rule gives him
the capability to add 3 to the power of any combat vehicle or shuttle
vehicle he pilots."

Note that this rule does not work the other way. Characters who
specifically add to vehicles or combat vehicles (e.g., AT-AT Driver)
may pilot starships (if they have a pilot icon), but they do not add
any power bonus.

---Mark Tuttle [address removed] wrote:
>
> 
> > Are 'starfighter' and 'combat vehicle' interchangeable when
> > deciding modifiers. <snip> Commander Luke Skywalker's game text is
> > something like "adds 3 to power of any starship he pilots and on
Rogue 1 adds
> > 2 to manuver"... So would that mean that even tho Rogue one isn't a
> > starfighter but because its a combat vehicle it gets the +3
modifier for
> > power?
> 
> 
> No, they're not really interchangeable. <snip>

From: Chris Klint
Subject: Re: Weekly Complaint
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000109153557/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/49986.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Weekly Complaint>Well, after tonight I am starting a regular feature for me on the listserv
>until regionals are finished. I am going to complain once a week about how
>much I hate the two odds cards and Asteroid Sanctuary.
>
>I realize there are ways to counter these cards, but they do far more than
>direct damage. They KILL all the fun I have in playing this game. Everytime
>I see them played, I want to scream. It means my opponent gets to do a lot of
>non-interaction the whole game. While I do all the work.. usually for naught,
>as his asteroid draws will eventually destroy my ships or he will out-
>Interrupt me in trying to Alter or Point Man the Asteroid Sanctuary. Odds
>usually involve hiding and the newest version that I loathe, the EBO/Odds
>deck, SUCKS because you can't get Ultimatum to work if you devote your forces
>to hunting down the cowards in Echo Base.
>
>Asteroid Sanctuary and Odds inserts still suck..

Don't they? Anyhow, while we're on this hopefully brief thread, I'd like
to take this opportunity to lodge a Standing Gripe (as opposed to a mere
Weekly Complaint) against the Light Side as a whole. Light's gone beyond
the "slippery" sense it had in Premiere to a just plain *slimy* nature
inherited, appropriately enough, from Dagobah. While most of Dagobah's
abusive cards have been dealt with in Cloud City and JP, the expansion
itself taught players that because interaction is an uncertain strategy,
non-interaction is the way to go.

 "The Dark Side of the Force, represented by the Galactic Empire, is
the
 side of the Force that tends to be more technically advanced, better
 equipped and the stronger aggressor. As a contrast with its rugged
 appearance, the Light Side, represented by the Rebel Alliance, has some
 surprising advantages. Using the power of the Force, they enhance
their
 defensive capabilities, maximize their resourcefulness and achieve a
 sense of honor that sets them apart from their glossier counterparts."

 -From the SW:CCG Rulebook

Does anyone here feel that these descriptions ought to be switched? Ask
yourself: Who has superior medical technology like Bacta Tanks and Bionic
Hands? The Light Side. Who keeps getting cards like Life Debt designed to
inflate the value of Mains N' Toys, both commercially and as an
eternally-viable deck? The Light Side. Who has all the adders that merely
specify the presence of two characters without requiring a defensive
battle, hence making Mains N' Toys the most versatile yet powerful
offensive strategy in the game? The Light Side.

Look at the Dark Side. What's its most crucial card today? Imperial
Decree-a card designed to enhance Dark's defensive capabilities and protect
it from the vicious Force drain poundings Light can dish out. Dark decks
have to face many more potential Light threats than vice versa; beating
Turbo Sanc and BoD, for example, with the same Dark deck is *much* more
difficult than it is for one Light deck to beat Walker Garrison and Dark
Deal. These factors combine to make Dark the honorable side to play. Why
*is* it that I never feel truly evil when I play as Dark? Why *is* it that
the Empire did so much Striking Back in the second film of the trilogy, yet
so little of it translated to the expansions?

Personally, I believe that individual expansions should more accurately
mirror the course of the movies. Not to the point where every card title
is a line quote that must be played immediately after the line before it,
but more in terms of mood. Light should constantly be in a fair extent of
danger from the Empire, which should rise to *mortal terror* in end-of-film
expansions like Cloud City and Death Star. Conversely, Dark should have to
keep up the pressure to find Rebels, but it should have more power; the
Empire I play with in the CCG isn't the one I see in the trilogy with
nominal control over most of the galaxy. Basically, the relative
*difficulty* of the game for each side should rise and fall dramatically to
echo the dramatic events of the trilogy. That hasn't been happening, and
Light keeps getting cards it doesn't need like Smoke Screen and Baragwins.

Well, that's my Standing Gripe-what's yours?

From: Mark Tuttle
Subject: Re: Hidden Weapons Q
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000110075158/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/49996.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Hidden Weapons QAt 01:15 AM 7/1/98 +0800, Kelvin Lok wrote:
>If I play Hidden Weapons against an opponent & draws a 2-3 (target
>immediately captured) or 6+ (target immediately lost), can that opponent
>fire back with a weapon (before he is captured/lost)?

Nope. They are "immediately captured".

>What if I draw a 4-5 (target 'hit') with Dr Evazan present. Can that
>opponent fire back in this case (before he is 'operated')?

Yes. The Interrupt is a declared action. Then they can fire, then you can
declare Evazan.

At 08:09 PM 6/30/98 PDT, Salacious Crumb wrote:
>Jason-
>
>>Do you have to alternate firing weapons? Or do you fire all your
>>weapons, then the opponent fires all his weapons?
>
>The player who initiated the battle fires first, then actions alternate 
>(I believe that applicable interrupts, like Hidden Weapons, may be 
>played instead of action). Be forewarned, though, I am not extremely 
>clear on the exact timing paradigm at work here.

Playing of interrupts IS an action, unless it's a response to a previous
action.

===== swccg-discussion message #53912 (yahoo) =====
Date: Fri Jul 31, 1998  6:08
pm
From: "Alex \"Gold 31\" Alcalde" [address removed]
Subject: TREPORT-Indy-Corellia-Regional (Short Version)
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20010509192606/http://groups.yahoo.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/message/53912

Sorry guys that it took me soooo long to get this done, but work has
been a pain in the @$$ :) There will be a more detailed version 
to the Discussion lists tommorrow.

HOOSIER TO WORLDS!!!

Alex Alcalde
Gold 31

Saturday July 25th marked the first time that a SWCCGG qualifier was
held within the state of Indiana, perhaps an oversight on Decipher's 
part. Regardless of the turnout would be Hoosiers were finally glad
to see thier very own regional.

As early aas 7:30 AM players began filing through the Holiday Inn
Beech Grove's doors to ready themselves for play. By the time the
smoke had cleared at 10 AM a solid sixty players were registered to
begin play. They came from as far away as Champaign IL, and 
Cincinnati, OH representing three of the five states that comprise the
Correlia region.

Notable figures seen in Indy included Squadron Members: Heath Scheiman,
John Sarnecki, Justin Stauffer and Joe Alread (where were all the
others?), Origins winner David Nanninga, there to encourage his good
friend Zach Cowie (2nd at Origins), MTG Junior Super Series player Jeremy
Davis, and Indiana's only Beginner Tournament winner Steve Tolin.

Running around for most of the day making rulings (yes, I probably missed
one or two out of 50+, but hey Im human too), I was able to get a
good look at the different decks and strategies being played. Decks
varied from old faithfuls of Scanning Crews and "Mains & Toys" to
Schrillng decks, Inserts, Asteroids, Tie Fighter decks, LS Space and but
one of the most common was Jabba's Palace decks.

By far the most anticipated matchup of the day was a rematch of the 1996
World Championship: Joe Alread vs Heath Scheiman (AKA battle of the SMs-
Joe managed to pull this one out by 5). Tournament play always brings out 
the best in players as their were many close games by 5 or less, but of
course there were also some considerable "blowouts," one by as many as
54!!

After the 3 rounds were over Tim Harvey (Ft Wayne) stood undefeated at
12 (+132) and was bound to face Steve Brentson (Lafayette) 10 (+79).
Incidently Tim is ranked 4th in Corellia behind Joe Alread, so it wasn't
much of a surprise to see Tim in the finals, and Steve's solid play 
earned him a berth in the final round.

On a side note, Zach Cowie and Geof Young both tied for 4th, in order 
to decide who would go home with the 4th place trophy rumor has it they 
Rochambod for it. 

The Final matchup two almost identical decks and playing styles against
each other and was rather enjoyable to watch (as all who stayed to 
watch will tell you). However, it seemed that Steve's luck had run out
against Tim's Light deck as Steve simply couldn't deploy anything early
on and Tim's Used Grimtasshs kept Steve on the defensive. Steve managed
though to break Tim's stranglehold and hit Tim with a Vader's Obession.
Outcome: Tim by 10.

The second game seemed to pickup right where the first left off with.
Getting out Obi early on Steve seemed to have the advantage....untill
Vader mad his appearence and a slicing they would go. Steve hung in there
valiantly untill an incredible Sense/Alter war took place, the likes I 
hadn't seen since before Controls came out. Outcome Tim by 38.

And so after plowing through his competition, Tim Harvey, finaly earns 
himself a berth to this year's World Championships. And as a result he 
probably becomes the first Hoosier to do so.

In Conclusion, I would like to thank those that made the Indy regional
possible. Decipher Inc., John Kaufeld the incredible help of Steve, Aaron
and his brother, and of course all the players in attendence, without 
you guys I wouldnt be writing this. 

For more info and pics on the Indy Regional check out the 
Rebel Gamers of Indiana Homepage. (http://come.to/rebelgamers).

1)Tim Harvey (Ft. Wayne, In) 12 (+132)
2)Steve Brentson (Lafayette, In) 10 (+79)

3)Oni Okotie (Indianapolis, In) 10 (+75)
4)Zach Cowie (Naperville, Il) 10 (+65)
4)Geof Young (Plainfield, In) 10 (+65)
6)Joe Mornout (Lafayette,In ) 10 (+32)
7)Donald Barrett (Indianapolis, In) 8 (+94)
8)Eric Olson (Orland Park, Il) 8 (+81)

--------------------------------------------------------------
 Alex Alcalde Gold 31

 First wave of starfighters to assault the Death Star.
 Many were lost in the Battle of Yavin."

From: Mark Tuttle
Subject: Re: RENNEK QUESTION (DECIPHER PROS PLEASE)
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000108014743/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/53934.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: RENNEK QUESTION (DECIPHER PROS PLEASE) does RENNEK's special ability allow you to use as many force as you want , meaning can you do this: 
 use 4 force to increase his power by 4  (1 turn) 

 then play Beru Stew and increase power (2 turn) then play Blue Milk and increase power (3 turn) 
 is this is what is meant by  

 three times per turn, may CUMULATIVELY use 1 force to increase power by 1 

You can only use Rennek's gametext to up his power up to three times per turn as it  states on his card.  That means you can pay up to three force, to increase his power  by up to a +3 for that turn.  So, if you pay 1, he's +1.  2, he's +2, 3, he's +3.

 Mark T., Decipher Net Rep

===== swccg-discussion message #53939 (egroups) =====
Date: 1:57 PM

Sat
1
Aug 98
From: Jesse J Chounard II
Subject: Re: (question) about pilots
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000108100146/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/53939.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: (question) about pilotsAt 03:51 PM 8/1/98 EDT, [address removed] wrote:
> I have a question for Decipher. If a pilot adds two power to a starrship,
>would it also add power to a vehicle?

I'm not official, but I think I know the answer.

If it is a combat vehicle (like a Blizzard Walker) you can add pilots
(up to the number listed on the card, everybody else sits in passeger
slots) and they will add the power they would add on a starship.

But, on transport vehicles (like a Skiff or Sandcrawler) they don't
add any power, unless their gametext says they add to vehicles.

However, on a different note, when you've got characters on an
enclosed vehicle, they don't add any power to the battle, while they
do on an open vehicle.

Jesse J Chounard II

===== swccg-discussion message #55378 (egroups) =====
Date: 12:12 AM

Tue
1
Sep 98
From: Michael Mikaelian
Subject: Re: Newbies readin' the rules
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000108181353/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/55378.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Newbies readin' the rulesI've found the best way to teach new players the game is to make 30-card

decks that are similar in design. Before playing take one of each card

type out (I usually avoid systems, starships and devices, since they are

basically the same as sites, characters and effects) and explain what all

the numbers, dots and icons mean. Make sure you have a rulebook on hand

for the turn order on the back cover. After explaining what the different

cards are, play the first game 'open handed,' with each player's hand

layed out on the table. Throughout the course of the game, you are

essentially playing both sides. The new player will probably jump in and

begin to take over the game in stages, as they begin to understand it. Let

them. Best of all, avoid teaching strategy. Let them make arbitrary plays,

and they will learn strategy.

If you pick up a copy of InQuest #30 (I think; I know it has Xena on the

cover), it has a SWCCG basic training article by yours truly (plug), which

includes two 30-card deck lists.

-Mike

Michael Mikaelian

[address removed]

swccg-discussion: Re: Re: Ewoks as Interrupts Well, I just can't resist a streaming topic!!!! 
 I can't stand Ewoks.  I don't feel teddy bears have a legitamate place or role in the Star Wars universe. Nevertheless, the ARE a part of the Star Wars universe. George Lucas found the wisdom to add walking toys to the character base of his story. This done, Ewoks must be given their fair share in the Endor edition of SWCCG. 
 I have always enjoyed the way Decipher designed game play around movie plot lines (e.g. Hoth Shields, Dark Deal, Dagobah w/ Yoda). I would think and hope that Decipher would do the same with our fuzzy friends. 
 Annoying as they are, they deserve a place. 

 Just my O'pinion 

Let's look at this in Reserve.  What if the Ewoks had come first and Jawas didn't  appear until ROTJ?  Would you guys be saying the same thing?  Probably.  The  Ewoks at least took out a squad of Troopers pretty much by themselves.  Did the  Jawas?  Nope.  Thus, give us a chance and you might be surprised what we can do  with them.

around for awhile now...

Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000109084220/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/55387.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: SensingMartijn Peeters wrote:

> 

> How do you know what part of an interrupt is part of it's initiating?

> Are there any clear rules about that? Like why you can sense Nabrun

> Leids after destiny is drawn and Force is paid en why you can't Sense

> a Sense after destiny is drawn. Please don't say "Because" or ".....

> ruled it that way", I need some clear rulings about this.

The initiation of any action includes three things:

1. Saying you're playing that game action.

2. Declaring any targets of that game action.

3. Paying any costs involved.

You can Sense Nabrun after destiny is drawn, because that destiny

determines part (3). You cannot Sense Sense after the destiny is drawn

because it had nothing to do with the above 3 things.

--

Doug Faust ("Moff Phrim")

[address removed]

weapons sub-phase. One nice thing about this timing structure is that
the initiator clearly has the advantage. I think that's only natural
and proper. Retwin can protect himself with cards like Collateral
Damage, Force Field, Human Shield (if Retwin has a captive), Overload
(played before the battle), Self-Destruct Mechanism (if Retwin has a
droid), etc.

===== swccg-discussion message #58691 (egroups) =====
Date: 12:35 AM

Thu

From: Kevin E. Allen
Subject: More Differential Ramblings
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000109133211/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/58691.html?

swccg-discussion: More Differential RamblingsCome on guys, just admit it. There's no perfect system.

 Say we do go to this percentage-based system, which honestly I
wouldn't even want to code into Tournamnt Manager, much less have to do
manually in a tournament. Even if you only use it in the end as a
tiebreaker... Let's say you face someone who, through most of the day,
wins with minimal differential. This keeps him at the low end of his
win bracket, facing lesser players, but still scoring wins. Now you
beat him - meanwhile, someone else beats someone who is a good player,
shoots himself up, then gets smacked down by the best and tumbles. You
score more for facing a "so-so" player, the other guy, who beats the
better player, doesn't get as good an advantage.

 In ANY situation, there's going to be problem cases. The major
objection which most people have voiced here is incorrect, though,
I think. The major problem, as stated, is that differential rewards
people for playing bad players. Two problems with this. First off,
it's not an accurate statement all the time. Just because I win with a
+40 doesn't mean my opponent was bad. I pulled a +40 against a
tournament-winner a few back. It just means I played a good game, maybe
he played a bad one or had a bad draw. Secondly, it is a necessary
evil, whether you like it or not. Something has to be done as a
tiebreaker, and using a different system to determine the winner than
you do to determine the matchups is begging for disaster. The Swiss
system does, indeed, have the potential to have some really screwy
matchups in the last game. Usually, though, top plays top through the
second-to-last game. It works out.

 Finally, keep in mind that the major objection - the "bad opponent
boost" - is self-correcting. If I play a bad opponent, and score a high
differential, I jump up. That means I will end up facing better
players. I've run more tournaments than I can count, and I've watched
the pairings so closely it's made my eyes hurt, for analysis into
Tournament Manager algorithms. Even a good player who hits the top
quick from such a boost will find himself besieged all day. And if he
manages to hold opponents off despite that position, doesn't he deserve
it anyway?

 One last thing... My disagreement with your opinion, Mike, doesn't
relegate my thoughts to the "Everyone has an opinion, but it's just an
opinion" category. Thinking you're wrong is perfectly valid. I could
just as easily say that you only thought it needed to be fixed because
it had cost you the win in a tournament and you're bitter. But that
wouldn't be very nice, now would it?

- Phoenix

===== swccg-discussion message #58692 (egroups) =====
Date: 5:29 AM

Thu
1
Oct 98
From: James Leiser
Subject: Re: (OT) Differential as Tiebreaker
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000109150834/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/58692.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: (OT) Differential as TiebreakerI don't believe first game pairings are the problem. If you institute a

non-random first game, then you can expect closer finishes in each match.

Since this will happen accross the board to even the lower ranked player's

games, when you apply the second game pairings you end up with the

mismatches. 

I think a better solution would be to run an additional round. The

problem with this is the amount of time it takes. A 2 round swiss seems

to be just enough time for the better players to come to the front, but

not enough time to let them play off. In the larger tournaments, like

regionals, a 4 round tournament would probably be better if you just look

at the statistics. The problem with this is that by game 6 most people

start to make mistakes from fatigue. If you run 8 games, all in one day

energy levels due to travel distance etc. may prove too significant in the

final results.

So what does all this boil down to. In my opinion 6 game tournaments tend

to balance the differential vs. wins ratio, while 4 game tournaments

reward differential by no allowing enough time for the wins to take on a

more significant weight.

Later,

James

On Wed, 30 Sep 1998, Mike Smith wrote:

> Following all these threads, it seems that differential is _not_ the

> source of the problem. The source of the problem appears to be the

> first game pairings, which are random.

> 

> I see two situations that can arise as a result of the first, random

> pairing. Many of the skilled players could end up playing less

> skilled players and all have very high differentials. Second (and

> perhaps worse), many of the skilled players could end up having the

> same allegiance (Dark or Light) at the same time. This will prevent

> them from playing against each other next time and could lead to

> stronger players again playing weaker players.

> 

> This is probably totally unworkable, but here's another suggestion. 

> Instead of randomizing the command cards at the beginning, order them

> by current rating.. So you start off playing someone of approximately

> equal skill. This may actually make the ratings more

> useful/important, although it puts the onus on players to know their

> current ratings.

===== swccg-discussion message #58695 (egroups) =====
Date: 6:41 AM

Thu
1

From: Mike Smith
Subject: Re: (OT) Differential as Tiebreaker
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000109195950/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/58695.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: (OT) Differential as TiebreakerThat's a good point; I hadn't thought of the second-game effects. It
seems, then, that no matter how you do it, you'll have at least one
game in the first round with the potential for mismatched games. The
mismatch could be in the first game with random pairings or in the
second game with non-random pairings.

Here is what I believe the best-case and worst-case outcomes are after
each game, in terms of percentage of undefeated players. The best
case occurs when the winners were evenly split between Light and Dark
in the first game of a round and face off against each other in the
second game of the round. The worst case can only happen if all the
first-game winners of any round were playing the same side, then they
all switch sides for game two of that round and win those games, too.

Game Best case Worst Case
 % undefeated % undefeated

 1 50 50
 2 25 50
 3 12.5 25
 4 6.25 25
 5 3.125 12.5
 6 1.5625 12.5

Given that you can't have partial people, that means that with 32
players or less, the best case is to have only 1 undefeated player
after 4 games. You could have as many as 8 undefeated among 32
players, but that would be very unlikely I'd think. I'm not sure what
the average might be. A lot depends on the allegiance assignment in
the first game.

With more than 32 players, it's impossible to have only 1 undefeated
player after 4 games, even in the best case. If you have more than 64
players, it's impossible to have just 1 undefeated player after 6 games.

the obscene destiny of a Baragwin deck, that has potential.

> Well... we'll just have to wait and see. I'm hoping that with EPP I can
> make a LS Space deck that can offer stiff resistance on the ground. Maybe?

 I don't know. The LS is just so card-inefficient in the air that
it's hard to say. Even though you only really need 2 Obis and 2 Han w/Gun
for a ground strike team, the space complement is going to be *HUGE* and
leave very little room for defense. I've not yet been convinced Haven
decks work, and that's basically all LS non-sector air has going for it.

> counterpart on the LS. I just think I may end up packing a Sentinel for
> it's versatility. Alas, it does lack immunity to attrition, but no one is
> perfect!

 Also great about it is that we finally have a non-BH ship that can
land and be piloted by anyone. Landing for free through the clouds is
interesting. Dagobah is looking worse and worse every set of images that
gets released.

> I think the coolest part is the Naboo reference. I guess this means that
> maybe the two games will be compatible. That would be so very cool indeed...

 I'm imagining the following scenario: Trilogy and Prequel cards have
identical mechanics and rule overlaps, but the cards cannot be mixed for
sanctioned tournament play. So there would be like, "Trilogy Swiss" and
"Prequel Swiss" - although if you wanted to just play fun games, you could
mix and match when playing friends. I worry that some of the Jedi based
cards may have been designed and balanced with a very low number of Jedi
in mind, but the prequels will have a drastically different Jedi count.
Imagine prequel Jedi (The Council) and Trilogy technology (T-47s and
T-65s...). Or, the Empire with Mandalorian Commandos and ISDs. Pretty
nuts... I think the game would begin suffering from Dragonball Syndrome at
that point.

From: Chris
Subject: A Plea for Help
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000306023701/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/62722.html?

swccg-discussion: A Plea for HelpTonight, I was at a haunted house at the Lions Club, all decked out in my

Greedo mask and tux. Poking out of my pocket were two Greedo action

figures: the new Power of the Force one, and my original figure that I've

had since I was 3 or so. By the time I made it through the house, I only

had one (and it WASN'T the old one). Somewhere in that house, I lost a

treasured part of my Star Wars existence: the only original figure I had

left and one of the main reasons I'm so addicted to Star Wars now.

I'm a Greedo fanatic--check out my email address, webpage, BBS handle and

Halloween costume if you don't believe me. And now I've lost one of the

things that have made me love him so much.

I left my phone number with the men from the club in case they find it,

so with any luck, my Greedo figure will be returned to me unharmed. I'm

asking you to offer a prayer to whatever deity you worship, or if you're

not religious, to wish me luck in this difficult time.

This may seem silly or trite to you, but I'm sure everybody has some

insignificant trinket that they adore. This Greedo figure has been with

me for 16 years: longer than my two sisters, longer than five of my dogs.

As corny as it may sound, it's very important to me.

Thanks for listening to the rant of a Greedo-less guy :(

Chris Caruso

about it." -- Sideshow Bob

Every card change in the game will be included in the Glossary which is set for release  in just a few weeks.

 3,      Whats the best counter to hidden weapons and All wrapped up? 

Well, All Wrapped Up is immune to Alter, so your best bet is to hide from bounty  hunters intent on capturing you and make sure you win those battles with as little  forfeiture as possible!  If AWU is on the table, you can't even Sense We Have A  Prisoner so my advice is the same.

swccg-discussion: More reason to buy local!In a message dated 98-11-08 03:09:43 EST, [address removed] writes:

<< hear hear! I totally agree. I am spending $8 more on a booster box.

Yippy.

 I'll gladly pay $8 to help keep a place open that let's me play SW there

until

 2 in the morning on Friday nights. I like your style.

 >>

As I have been preaching to support your local comic book store lately,

another point has popped into my head. Many of these internet places cannot be

trusted. Sure you send your money, and sure you get your box, but what are in

the packs? (While I'm on this, if you buy local, you'll have your box first

too) I have heard many a story, and it has been proven that, there is a

pattern to packs in a box. Many of these mail order or internet stores can

find the pattern, take the good cards, put many packs from a different box in,

and give you junk! Even if there is no apparent pattern, they can open packs

utnil getting two mains or so, then throw some more trash packs that are left

into another box, and re-seal it. And yes, it is very easy to re-seal a box

with the Decipher wrapping.

Nick

From: Adam East
Subject: What's your largest win in a single battle ?
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000108112340/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/63777.html?

swccg-discussion: What's your largest win in a single battle ?Played a game on the weekend with my Tatooine/Snivvian/Nightfall deck

and after 5 or so turns, all the cards fell my way. I was able to

convert the DS AC (He was playing an AC Alien Representatives deck) and

had Arleil Schous and Tessek at the AC with Bo Shuda in play. He had

only managed to get out Myo at the AC with 2 Abyssins at a neighbouring

JP site (despite having a heap of sites out.)

I deployed Nightfall, 2X-3KPR, Luke with Anakin's Stick, Tamteel, KFC, a

couple of Snivvians, a Talz and some Rebel spies all for the grand cost

of 5 force in the same turn. A battle ensued ... of course :) He didn't

have anything in hand to help him out.

The final tally after destiny draws came to a win for me (Obviously ...)

with the DS copping a battle damage of 35 (and one of the Abyssians had

been sticked for forfeit=0). That's the biggest outcome I've ever had in

my favour. I'm sure others have seen bigger wins and I'd like to hear

about it, so;

What's the biggest battle damage you've seen in one battle at a

Tournament game, and how was it set up ?

Adam East

massive abuses goes, I'd be surprised as the couple of hundred list brains
are yet to find one.... (and they can certainly take time)

advertise the biggest set in the game's history as they wish. And I reckon
that if you asked 56 (54?) Qualifiers if they wanted to just replay their
Regional games (i.e. beware of Tat:JP/ETE. Eloms, Skrillings, Baragwins,

set (for free), and throw the playing field wide open - then I reckon it
would have been a pretty healthy vote for the latter (especially amongst
those who are truly original deckmakers, as opposed to those who pick up
the already proven strongest deck at the time).

swccg-discussion: Problem with Inserts in Glossary edition Rules 2.Tom, Chuck, Mark, and Allen,
 There is a problem with the Glossary 2.0 Rules. It states that inserts
when inserted in your opponents deck go face down(the mistake) and
inserts that you insert in your own deck go face up. Here is the
problem. I am playing a sabaac deck. My opponent is playing inserts. I
have won like 3 of his starships and I have an insert in my deck. One of
2 things has to happen. When I activate, do I check each card as I come
to a card with a differnt backing for the interrupt? or do I put the
insert face up the way it should have been and now know the difference
between the cards? What made you want to go and change the rule? I hope
this doesnt mess up a future game mechanic.

=-=-=-=-=-=-=-
This is interesting one.

My opinion?
From glossary: "insert card - any card which instructrs you to insert it
into a reserve deck". Thus an insert inserted or Treva Horned into your
reserve deck is on both counts an "insert card"

Later in the glossary:
"Whenever one of your opponents cards comes to the top of your reserve
deck, you must reveal it..... if it is an insert card, resolve it
immediately.

Thus by my reckoning, a NTMTO Treva Horned into a DS deck will still make
the DS take damage when it is revealed, and it will then be lost!!!!

A rather inefficient way of getting your inserts off (it goes into force
pile, must then e used, and then is at the bottom of the reserve awaiting
to be brought up again...). And Treva is destiny 2.... but hey.... if you
*really* wanna do it...

Cheers,
Juz

From: Kevin E. Allen
Subject: Why the Glossary isn't a Dictionary
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000109100326/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/65212.html?

swccg-discussion: Why the Glossary isn't a DictionaryChuck has recently said, in defense of the Glossary's immense size,
that it's "Like a dictionary," the idea being that when you have a
question you go look something up.

 Well, that works okay for TDs. It works very well when you have
questions or problems as to how something is played. What about the
rest of the time??

 Let's say, for the sake of argument, that I'm a new player. I just
sprung the cash for a few starters of Premiere and SE, I've got the new
rules. I read through, my friend and I play a little, I teach him how
to play. Okay... Now, I find this really rocking card called "Tallon
Roll" that lets my weak little TIEs stand up to those overpowered
X-Wings - well, a little, at least. Especially with those 4 TIE
Defenders I pulled from the SE starters. So I trade with my friend
after he gets some, and I put together a nice TIE Defender/Tallon Roll
deck and head to my first tournament, that I saw advertised.

 "You can't do that." That's what my opponent says to me when I
Tallon Roll his first X-Wing. My Defenders proceed to get jumped by 8
S-foiled X-Wings with A Few Maneuvers thrown in, I lose a battle by
about 38 Force. Game over. Oh, sure, it's in the glossary. What good
does that do me now?

 Sure, I'll never make that mistake again. But how many of those are
there in the glossary? How many of those am I going to put up with
before I get fed up and quit the game?

 The Glossary is NOT a dictionary. Nobody reads straight through a
dictionary. When you go to a dictionary YOU KNOW WHAT YOU'RE LOOKING
FOR. Ever try to look up "sagaciousness" when you didn't know how it
was spelled? How are you supposed to know what to look for in the
glossary until you've had some experienced player point it out, probably
at your expense in a tournament? Nor is it purely for reference,
either. From what I've heard here many of the core concepts of the game
are in there. How am I to learn what I need to know short of reading it
cover to cover?

 Though I intend to stay on the list, and continue my "crusade", I am
shelving the game for a while. Perhaps forever. This rewrite, and the
general power increase in the cards, seems to me a very
poorly-considered and unnecessary move on Decipher's part, and only time
will tell what it does to the game. I honestly hope I'm wrong. I fear
I am right.

 BTW, Mark - We'll keep telling you what we think of errata until you
decide to ban us. Once again, Decipher set their own tune. Nobody
holds politicians to broken promises these days. Maybe we'll take a
shot at holding you to them.

- Phoenix

From: Kevin E. Allen
Subject: Some Special Edition Statistics
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000109163111/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/65481.html?

swccg-discussion: Some Special Edition StatisticsI spent a little time going through the SE spoiler list today,
looking for numbers to support some thoughts I had been having on the
set. They follow below, with explanation.

 Special Edition is a double-sized set, at 324 cards. Though I didn't
count to prove it, I am assuming that it maintains the past pattern of
an even split of light and dark cards, meaning there are 162 of each.
Most people probably figure that with a double-sized set, that means
there are twice as many ideas and new cards as in any other set. In
actuality, this is not hardly true. The numbers below are, if anything,
low, as I may have missed some. Consider the following :

 Of the 162 Light Side cards, 49 are "twinned" to the Dark Side. This
means there is a card for the DS which is, for all intents and purposes,
identical in its primary use, if not entirely. 30% - well more than 1
in 4. This includes the generic locations, but does not include
specific locations or Operatives. Adding those two to the counts brings
the "twin" total to a whopping 63, or 39% - considerably more than 1 in
3. This is considering ONLY cards in this set. Rebel Ambush, for
instance, twins Trooper Assault from a previous set, and is not
counted. So, end result is that in a set of 324 cards, there are, in
actuality, fewer than 200 actual new cards, JUST WITHIN THE SET.
Inclusion of the out-of-set twins would reduce the number even further.

 The set includes 7 new Rebel pilots, whose only difference is the
system they apply their Force drain bonus to and the fighter they match
to.

 The set includes 7 new Rebel fighters and 1 new unique Snowspeeder.
All are fundamentally identical, with the only difference being, again,
the system they apply their bonus to and which weapon they gain the
advantage for. Two deviations do happen here : The Gold fighters
retrieve 1 Force when deployed to the right place, whereas Red fighters
deploy free. Also, Red 8 and Red 9 provide a bonus to-hit rather than
free deploy and firing.

 The Light Side has 26 Interrupts in the set. 17 of those - almost 2
in 3 - are starship related.

 Each side has 3 Celebration/Occupation cards - IDENTICAL cards with
the exception of which system they deploy to. Any reason why it
couldn't have been "Planetary Occupation" or "Celebration Of Freedom"
which deployed to Coruscant, Bespin, or Tatooine?

 Each side has approximately 10 Operatives, whose only difference is
the system they relate to, and what other item of presence gives their
bonus. No differences in bonus, or deploy, or forfeit, or power....
Nothing.

 There are numerous other instances which exist but I didn't bother to
count. So, this "revolutionary, double-sized" set is really anything
but. Apart from a massive increase in the general power of the cards,
the only thing double-sized about the set is how much money you're going
to have to spend to complete it.

 Dissenting comments always welcome, but please include facts to
counter those above.

- Phoenix

and so far it's looking like every fear I had from SE is coming to pass,
though perhaps in a manner I hadn't expected. Everyone said to wait and
see SE played... Well, now we're seeing it. What are we seeing?

 Players draining for 6 on the second turn?

 Flipping Objectives on the first turn?

 Games being decided by who has the better opening hand, and in some
cases who goes first?

 Am I the only one who thinks this is a bad thing? Weren't the first
and third reasons there the reason that Asteroid Sanctuary was
errata'ed?

swccg-discussion: OperativesOkay there is alot of fuss over this but c'mon they aren't that bad. If you

play a good deck they can be shut down. A couple of cards that work nicely are

A Bright Center and The Planet Farthest from. With these their drains at the

sites are reduced to 5. A Novice couls handle 5 force a turn. And operatives

aren't the strongest of characters, How bout 4 Abyssins with a Tooex Elising

in after you've deployed Nightfall? There are many ways to beat them and each

person should find a style that suits them. At least play in 2 or 3

tournaments and see how it goes. Oh and what about the EPPs??? They will work

very nicely in space decks playing this type of deck as you can use them to

mop up the operatives and if you are lucky flip the objective. Anyway May the

Force Be With You.

From: Eric Hatcher
Subject: SE Operatives getting mobbed?
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000109230008/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/65739.html?

swccg-discussion: SE Operatives getting mobbed?Well,

 Let's see... The Special Edition expansion has been publicly
available for a little over 5 days, and it seems that everyone is
ready to jump on their horses, gather up the possey, and hang a few
operatives. Am I the only one who thinks that it might be a little
early to begin bashing Decipher for destroying the game we have all
come to know and love. It just seems to me that there hasn't been
enough time to take into account every possible means to deal this
powerful deck archetype.

 Perhaps it is something which will need to be errataed, like
Asteroid Sanctuary, or perhaps it will be something that will be dealt
with through strategy, like the Jabba's Palace manipulator decks. In
our area, I recall these decks becoming very popular, and even
referred to as broken by a few people. However, people adopted to the
situation. The throne room and Bomarr Monks became quite popular. JP
Manipulator decks declined, and due to this, so did the Monk Decks.

 That's pretty much my two cents on the whole matter. No
flaming is intended, and I hope none is inferred. I just grew weary of
reading so many negative posts on the subject, and thought I'd try to
inject a bit of optimism.

 Party on,

 Eric Hatcher

Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000110035353/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/66025.html?

swccg-discussion: Couple of anti Ops cards I just thought ofKevin posted yesterday that we should find cards that help

against Ops rather than entire deck themes. I agree.

Here are some I thought of. Remember that you've gotta

have some sort of battle strategy to have a chance, and

that is, in itself, a dangerous way to go against Operatives.

(But if you try to outdrain them, you're gonna lose.)

These are a little more specific on where you can use them,

but when you're just trying to stay alive, anything is helpful.

(Especially if it's early game, and you get a couple of the

quick "kill an operative" cards and manage to make them flip

back over, negating their retrieval for a little bit.

And, please don't anybody send me messages like "That card

won't work, cause Ops are too powerful." Please be constructive.

LS:

Fallen Portal (at the docking bay)

You can nuke 2 of them, pretty easily, which will probably clear

the site early on, saving you a force drain of 2.

Undercover, Narrow Escape, and Arcona

This might be able to use these to slow them down a little

while you set up something.

Artillery Remote

Set up an Artillery Weapon, and then you can fight at one

site while nuking the guys at another every control phase.

LS & DS:

Sandwhirl (at the Desert)

I dunno about everybody else, but I've had alot of trouble 

fitting any Controls into an Ops deck. So it is possible

that with them playing few (or no) Controls, that the

Sandwhirl could keep the site clear for a long time.

And even if they have a Control, it's a one to one card loss,

which isn't bad.

Maybe these will lead to somebody else thinking of some.

From: Marc Cosgrove
Subject: Dealing with abuse strategies
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000110053228/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/66055.html?

swccg-discussion: Dealing with abuse strategiesok a lot of people are unhappy about ops decks, I am somewhat too...

abuse of course is a subjective term, usually the person doing the abuse
doesn't see it as abusive...

now lets examine some of the history of abuse decks...

Pre Culmulative Rule Era
------------------------

I Have You Now/The Force is Strong With This One abuse - 5 + of each
played per battle (to add up to 10 battle destiny)

Jedi Presence Abuse - 3 or 4 multiple copies played during a single
battle (I have heard of times where as many as 8 were played at once)

Revoloution abuse - 8 + revoloutions

It Could be worse abuse - 6 + It could be worses

Multiple mains abuse - 4 + of each or certain mains (as many as 6 + in
some decks) (still exists to this day)

Post Culmulative Abuses
-----------------------

Pre AS sanc revision abuse
--------------------------
20 + asteroids + 15 + asteroid sancs (in some cases as many as 30
asteroids + 20 asteroid sancs)

Post AS sanc revision abuse
---------------------------
Asteroid sanctuary - requires large #s of multiple copies of cards to
work (10 + asteroids, 10 + ships, 10 + sancs in some cases)

Numbers - requires slightly fewer #s of multiples but was still centered
on having lots of multiples (often 8 + NTMTOs)

Dagobah Clouds - didn't need many multiples but still needed a fairly
good number of multiples to have any speed

Dagobah On the Edge retrieval - 4 to 6+ On the Edge

Trooper Assault Abuse - 15 + stormies?

SITD Dark Deal abuse - multiple copies of CC sites, multiples of low
deploy characters

Windchimes abuse - 15 + eloms...

Abyssins abuse - 15 + abyssins...

Operative abuse - 15 + operatives...

Snow/sand Speeder abuse - 15 +

Xwing abuse - 15 +

Baragwin abuse - 10 + devices

Infinite retrieval abuse - multiple copies???

anyone else see a pattern forming?

it's possible that some of these overwhelmingly abusive and or cheesy
strategies could be dealt with by restriciting the # of multiples of a
certain card in your deck... perhaps 10 would leave room enough for a
deck to function? 

I don't know, but the trend I see is using large numbers of a certain
card to the "abuse" point... even limiting operatives to ten per deck
would only slightly lessen the damage of an operative deck but perhaps
that would be enough?

A limit on multiple copies is something I think that we've needed for a
very long time. Anyone else have any thoughts on this?

OCF/Ozzel's Cousin Fred/Marc Cosgrove

Subject: Operatives
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000110102734/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/66569.html?

swccg-discussion: OperativesAt the risk of getting flamed (go buy your bags of rocks & gravel just down

the road) full marks to Stuart Jones one of the World Championship finalists

who plans on playing in our upcoming tournament with anti - operatives decks

that remain flexible enough to compete against other deck types. All this

talk about boycotting tournaments until Decipher does something about

Operatives is disappointing. Whilst SWCCG is a religion we are all

passionate about, sometimes you just have to stand back and say "it is just

a game after all". I'm currently working on decks which can beat operatives

but remain flexible enough to go up against other deck types.

My current light deck revolves around starting with Local Uprising, Tatooine

System, the Farm and Bright Centre of the Universe. Combined with The Gift,

Mains, SAC and Harvest I'm determined to make it work. On the dark side, ISB

combined with 5 Servant Droids and Planet its Furthest From etc is the other

idea I have. Admittedly its early days for my dark deck but lets find ways

to beat ops but remain deck flexible. Too hard I hear you say well time will

tell and if it is Decipher I'm sure will do something about it.

Personally I dont like Operatives and the "bunker" mentality the theme

encourages. Just as I didnt like Odds for similar reasons however Decipher

have given us some protection from them with Ultimatum & Resistance. Give me

interaction any day Baby! Until we get some final word from Decipher, lets

design some decks that meet the anti ops/deck flexible criteria and take

them to the tournaments and kick those no-brainer degenerate Op's decks.

Personally wouldnt that be more satisfying and inspirational than boycotting

tournaments until Decipher does something about the Op's strategy (assuming

they will?)

Just my 2 cents worth

Terry Lyons

"Restrict Deployment Restrictions"

From: Steve White
Subject: My attempt at a DS Anti-Op deck
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000110152525/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/66572.html?

swccg-discussion: My attempt at a DS Anti-Op deckI don't know about this one. It seems like it would be okay. It's not too
powerful. Just wanted to see what you guys thought. It's really cheap to
make, just seven rares, and two of them are pretty easily replaceable. It
also utilizes some of the "Binder Fodder" of older days. It looks like it
would be fun to play, whether it wins or not. If you're not playing an
Operative deck, it sounds fun just to see an army of Stormtroopers led into
battle against Darth Vader, or a squadron of TIEs duking it out with
X-Wings. All while dreading the appearance of the Death Star, ever closing
in. I don't know, it sounds like a fun deck, if not a tourney winner. 

Starting

Ralltiir Operations/In The Hands Of The Empire
Ralltiir

Locations

Clouds xxx X 3
Death Star
Desert x
Forest x
Jungle x
Spaceport City x
Spaceport Docking Bay x
Spaceport Prefect's Office x
Spaceport Street x
Swamp x

Characters

Darth Vader
Grand Moff Tarkin
Stormtrooper X 8

Starships

TIE Assault Squadron X 5
TIE Fighter X 5

Interrupts

All Power To Weapons X 5
Trooper Assault X 5

Effects

Imerial Decree X 8
Resistance X 5

Epic Event

CPI X 3

The strategy is to get Ralltiir out, and hopefully, on the first turn you
will be able to get another planet site on Ralltiir, and deploy ships to
Ralltiir, and whatever site your opponent played, plus a stormtrooper on a
Ralltiir site, this will allow Imperial Decree to take effect. If this
doesn't happen, you will at least get a Resistance out, and be able to use
that to lower force drain until you can. Vader and Tarkin can be used to
deploy the Stormtroopers for free.

If your opponent is using a system where you have no presence, use the
Clouds xxx to deploy starfighters to his system. Hopefully, you will be
able to get a steady force drain at Ralltiir and his system, and deploy
some troopers and Vader. At least this way their force drain will not be as
great as yours.

Eventually, you will get your Death Star out, and you're just a CPI away
from getting rid of all his Operative power. Then he has to fight you on
your own turf.

Anyway, I'm sure there are lots of things I missed, but hopefully you will
help me hone it down to fight those pesky Operatives, at least the Light
Side ones.

The biggest problem I can see is that your opponent uses Ralltiir
operatives. The best way to combat this would be to get some other planet
systems in, or pray you get the Death Star right away, and can control
Ralltir and the Death Star so Imperial Decree takes effect.

Tear it apart fellas.

-Steve White

From: John D Borders
Subject: Re: Operatives and Solutions
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000110171617/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/66573.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Operatives and SolutionsHow about this as a possible erratta for 2 cards.

The objectives
Imperial Occupation
Local Uprising

Eratta for BOTH sides of the above cards.

"Your operatives are deploy +2"

The biggest problem with the operatives is their speed. This eratta
doesnt prevent people from playing them, it pretty much prevents a first
turn flip. Making it necessary for a 2nd or 3rd turn flip. After their
speed the problem was deploy/power ratio. This should even that out. 
They wont be able to drop 7 operatives for 7 force at 5 or 6 power each.
If people choose to play WITHOUT the objectives, the operatives lose
their power/forfeit bonuses making them not too powerful. It also keeps
from having to eratta 20 more cards(10 operatives per side). Just 2
cards are necessary.

What do you all think of this? I would like feedback.

John Borders - [address removed]

From: Steve White
Subject: Revised! My attempt at a DS Anti-Op deck ((Ignore
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000108000903/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/66574.html?

swccg-discussion: Revised! My attempt at a DS Anti-Op deck ((IgnoreI don't know about this one. It seems like it would be okay. It's not too
powerful. Just wanted to see what you guys thought. It's really cheap to
make, just seven rares, and two of them are pretty easily replaceable. It
also utilizes some of the "Binder Fodder" of older days. It looks like it
would be fun to play, whether it wins or not. If you're not playing an
Operative deck, it sounds fun just to see an army of Stormtroopers led into
battle against Darth Vader, or a squadron of TIEs duking it out with
X-Wings. All while dreading the appearance of the Death Star, ever closing
in. I don't know, it sounds like a fun deck, if not a tourney winner. 

***I added some stuff, I felt like an idiot, and put in a Twi'lek Advisor,
like I should have from the start. That left some room for other cards. I
also added Executor, just for power and the ability to really sock it to
them in space if needed. It adds a rare, but oh-well. Still under ten
rares.***

Starting

Ralltiir Operations/In The Hands Of The Empire
Ralltiir
Twi'lek Advisor
Imperial Decree

Locations

Bespin
Clouds xxx X 3
Dantooine
Death Star
Desert x
Forest x
Hoth
Jungle x
Spaceport City x
Spaceport Docking Bay x
Spaceport Prefect's Office x
Spaceport Street x
Swamp x

Characters

Darth Vader
Grand Moff Tarkin
Stormtrooper X 10

Starships

Executor
TIE Assault Squadron X 5
TIE Fighter X 5

Interrupts

All Power To Weapons X 5
Trooper Assault X 5

Effects

Resistance X 5

Epic Event

CPI X 3

 The strategy is to get Ralltiir out, and hopefully, on the first turn you
will be able to get another planet site on Ralltiir, and deploy ships to
Ralltiir, and whatever site your opponent played, plus a stormtrooper on a
Ralltiir site, this will allow Imperial Decree to take effect. (If you're
lucky) This should at least be able to happen by turn two. (The
stormtrooper and the site are gauranteed by turn two, due to the Objective,
the TIE fighters will need to be lucky, but you should only need two)

 If this doesn't happen, you should at least get a Resistance out, and be
able to use that to lower force drain until you can. Vader and Tarkin can
be used to deploy the Stormtroopers for free.

 By turn three, you should be able to flip the Objective, realistically
four at the latest. If you get incredibly unlucky (you never draw a
stormtrooper or any characters) it will take six turns. This is a ludicrous
possibility though. It won't happen.

 Once you get Imperial decree, Resistance, and the Objective flipped, there
will be no fource drai bonuses for the LS, and all force drains will be
reduced to one. (I think, they will be reduced to two by Resistance, and
then reduced by one by the Objective, if this is wrong, they won't be more
than two anyway, and that will at least even the odds.)

 If your opponent is using a system where you have no presence, use the
Clouds xxx to deploy starfighters to his system. Hopefully, you will be
able to get a steady force drain at Ralltiir and his system, and deploy
some troopers and Vader. At least this way their force drain will not be as
great as yours.

 This effectively reverses his strategy, the planet he wants is impossible
to use, because there is little to no force drain for him. He has to fight
you, and your Vader, Tarkin, Stormtroopers, Trooper Assaults, all with his
20 Operatives, that aren't on their home planet. Nice, huh? You might be
able to destroy them if he transports them with a starship by blasting that
away with your TIEs, but that would be foolish on his part.

 Eventually, you will get your Death Star out, and you're just a CPI away
from getting rid of all his Operative power. Then he has to fight you on
your own turf.

 Anyway, I'm sure there are lots of things I missed, but hopefully you will
help me hone it down to fight those pesky Operatives, at least the Light
Side ones.

 The biggest problem I can see is that your opponent uses Ralltiir
operatives. The best way to combat this would be to get some other planet
systems in, or pray you get the Death Star right away, and can control
Ralltir and the Death Star so Imperial Decree takes effect.

Tear it apart fellas.

-Steve White

'van', 'der' and 'Meer' are really 3 dutch words:

'van' is translated in English as: 'of'
'der' is a dutch/german word, it means 'the'
'Meer' is a large body of water: 'Lake'

So, my name would in english be:

Johan of the Lake

From: Michael Girard
Subject: operatives don't need to be changed
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000108131809/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/66639.html?

swccg-discussion: operatives don't need to be changedwhy? operatives don't need to be errated. it is the deck to beat since a
new set has come out. i am sure these are the same people who were griping
that dagobah wasn't "fair" and it needed to be changed. operatives are just
a phase, kinda like asteroids, eloms, jp:ete, skirrlings and every other
tourney abused deck that has come across the board. the deck can be b eat
with very few cards subsituted into a deck. the reason everyone is
complaining is because they are stuck in the "mains and toys" mindset.
People need to go back and start looking at other cards from other sets.
Players need to expand their horizons and start to think creatively when
building decks. the anti-ops solution is farless luminous then having to
play the anti-jp strategy, you don't need to start out with certain cards
that automatically give you the upper hand in beating the deck, you just
need to include cards in your deck, and if they happen to be playing ops
then you just start with the specified cards. These "starting" cards have
diversified the game so much that you can change your deck style (if it is
versitile enough) right from the beginning of the game.
The solution to everyone's ops problems is not changing cards, it is
changing your way of thinking/deck building.

Michael Girard
Worlds Qualifier, first day chump
"What your playing ops, you just made my day, prepare to be annhilated"
