Thu
3
Dec 98
From: Kevin E. Allen
Subject: State Of The Game
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000108230241/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/67190.html?

swccg-discussion: State Of The GameRay asked what everyone's opinions on the general state of the game
were. He had focused it more towards the starting-game environment, but
I'd like to address the whole thing.

 One of the things I'd always loved about Star Wars over other CCGs
was the depth, the level of interaction and strategy with the cards on
the table, the thought that had to go into everything. Magic players
sneer at Star Wars and gloat that they can play four games of Magic in
the time it takes to play one game of Star Wars. I always considered
this to be to their detriment, not mine.

 The feel of the game started to change with Dagobah. Poorly designed
cards, and poor playtesting of those cards, began a wave of change to
less interactive strategies. Games were being won not only without ever
battling - this was always possible in a drain race - but without the
POSSIBILITY of battling. Odds decks, Dagobah drain decks, the infamous
"Feldman" deck, all were built to win without any interaction with the
opponent.

 I'll diverge quit a bit into other games I've played, which may seem
a little off-topic but is really relevant. Why? Because the failings
of those other games, which Star Wars avoided, was what made Star Wars a
good game. Star Trek suffered horribly from the non-interactivity of
the game. Though the licensing problems and slow product release
undoubtedly hindered the game a lot, in the end it was, quite simply, a
pretty boring game to play. You could just as easily have played two
solitaire games and timed each other, and whoever finished faster won.
Star Wars started to get like that.

 The problems with Dagobah didn't take long to surface, and if you
disagree that they were problems (I'm sure many do), I would point to
Decipher's attempts, over the course of three expansion sets, to fix
those problems and encourage interaction. Eventually, though,
interaction pushed its way back into the game, and we could once more
have massive battles between the lumbering Imperial walkers and the
noble Jedi wielding their lightsabers.

 Now, though, the game has taken a different turn. I think it started
with the evolution of Jabba's Palace/Expand The Empire. The change is,
in a word, speed.

 All of a sudden the Dark Side has a technique which gives them a 9:2
Force advantage on the first turn, without any real card play on their
part. It's just there. Counters existed, but it happened so fast that
your choices were severely limited. You basically had to either play
with the Throne Room and Monks to cancel the text, a way to Alter the
ETE early, or enough sites of your own to match their generation, which
put you at a severe disadvantage since you had to draw yours while
theirs were automatic. Over the course of two tournaments I saw an
80:30 advantage in wins to the Dark Side, and LS decks became utterly
one-dimensional. I believe at one tournament, out of 26 players, about
20 or so had Expanding Palaces. To this day I think that not fixing
this was one of Decipher's critical errors.

 The speed issue has gotten even worse now that Special Edition has
arrived. On one hand, the Objectives are great cards. They allow for
strategies that were very storyline-oriented, but location-intensive, to
work. They also, in many cases, encourage the use of older, ignored
rules such as Docking Bag Transit.

 On the other hand, though, the speed issue has been pushed to new
heights. Operatives win in 4-5 turns. Pacific Blue 3 is so-named
because if the LS can't put a VERY solid defense out by turn 3, they're
dead. Hidden Base pulls the key LS space drain systems one a turn, then
locks down an already expensive Dark Side. Raithal Operations allows
you the draw-any-card of Jedi Test #4 after only 2-3 turns, and even my
own favorite Jedi Training has met new heights of speed by potentially
graduating Luke by turn 7-8. It says a lot that that's not fast enough
any more.

 I suggested a few days ago that Operatives be errata'ed to a 4 deploy
to help fix them. I had two people tell me that would make them utterly
useless. Why? Because I can't drop six of them in one turn? One
person even replied to me "they would only be able to play one and draw
three cards in a turn, and that defeats the whole point of non-unique
aliens". Just because it's non-unique, you have to have 12 of them on
the table?

 When First Contact came out I started playing Star Trek again. Here
was all the interaction I'd been looking for! Playing a Borg
Assimilator, I went after the opponent for all he was worth. I
assimilated characters, planets, and disrupted the timeline, it was a
blast. Then Star Trek picked up a little in our area, and things got
competetive. I couldn't play it any more, because my opponent was
completing three missions before I even got moving, much less was able
to get to where they were and attack them. Games typically finished in
5-6 turns, and it was right back to timed solitaire.

 Now I see the same thing happening in Star Wars. When the game moves
this fast, it discourages interaction - it's all an ultra-speed drain
race, and if I stumble even a little bit, I'm dead. The "epic" cards
which make Star Wars Star Wars - Walkers, Star Destroyers, Vader,
Corellian Corvettes - have no place in this new universe because they're
simply too expensive. Instead we have swarms of X-wings that overpower
Star Destroyers in a one-on-one fight. We have 12 ISB Agents deploying
for free. We've got Operatives that either deploy and win the game by
turn 5, or don't, and everyone knows the outcome and it's just a matter
of playing it out.

 I know what a lot of people would say : "That's progress", "You just
don't like change", "You just don't like it because you can't beat it".
The first may be true, the second and third aren't. I can beat it, but
it's really pretty simple how I look at this.

 If this were the game I'd seen in a store three years ago, if I'd had
to buy four starter decks to get enough to play both sides, and
struggled through the rules... If I'd done all that for this game, I
would never have started playing.

 Ironically, I think Star Wars has finally succeeded in what is always
wanted.

 Star Wars has finally caught up to Magic.

- Phoenix

From: Mark Tuttle
Subject: Re: Hello,newbie here with a few ???
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/20000109070549/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/67532.html?

swccg-discussion: Re: Hello,newbie here with a few ???> Death Star Assault Squadron from SE:The card says that it has
> power=3,but in the text it says adds 9 to power of Vader's Custom
> tie,Black 2 and Black 3.Why, then, isn't the card power=12 instead of
> 3,when the glossary says that you can't replace this card with the
> others.likewise the Obsidian Squadron Tie and Black Squadron Tie says
> power=1,but permanent pilot adds 2 ability and 2 power.Why aren't they
> just power=3 instead.What am i missing?

You add the bonuses that are listed so the DSAS is indeed power 12. We 
just wanted to be able to reflect the base power of the Squadron and the 
bonuses seperately in case something might cause those bonuses to be 
lost.

> One more ?.If Luke and Cpt.Han
> are on the Falcon,do they both add power "to anyting he pilots",because
> they are both pilots,and Falcon can carry two piots?Is this how it
> works?

If a starship has multiple pilot slots, then you may have that many different 
pilots, all adding their respective bonuses.

swccg-discussion: Re: destiny questions> When a card refers to "add one destiny," does that mean add one to the
> destiny draw or does it mean take an extra draw? What if there wasn't
> any destiny draw to begin with?

It means to make an additional destiny draw. If it said "add +1 to destiny", 
then it would mean you would add +1 to the one destiny that you drew.

swccg-discussion: Re: Tractored X-wingsIn a rather unusual response, I quote myself to all three of the
responses I got on my Tractored X-wings post. This is appropriate,
since every one of them said the same thing, and none of them bothered
to look at what I said, much less the actual issue :

> I would also point out that, barring the play of some Interrupt,
> Tractor Beams are used AFTER the battle. If your Star Destroyer
takes

 I KNOW there are ways to do it otherwise. Emphasis "barring the play
of some Interrupt". None of them are the Tractor Beam itself, and
Lennox can only be in on place at one time, and can only be used during
your Control Phase, which helps not at all when 50 power worth of
X-wings deploy and beat the living snot out of you. You would need far
better than a 1:1 ratio of cards to capture 3 X-wings in a turn, and
let's not forget that you've got to beat their maneuver of 4 with a
single destiny draw. And I'll say it now : Yes, I KNOW there are ways
to improve that too, using YET ANOTHER Interrupt. If you want to go
ahead and build a whole deck around Tractor Beams you're welcome to, it
still won't come anywhere close to dealing with the raw power that the
X-wings can drop.

 You guys really should look at the big picture before offering up all
kinds of nifty solutions. One-card fixes are wonderful until you
actually try to play them.

- Phoenix

swccg-discussion: Re: A quick question on Starting InterruptsOn Sat, 5 Dec 1998 [address removed] wrote:

> Hi,
> Can you use "Don't Tread On Me" 3 times after you declare your starting
> location to pull all 3 of the effects listed on your card to get them out of

> choice out?

You can only play one Starting Interrupt, so take your pick of DTOM or
Signal, but not both. You may use DTOM once to pull all three Effects
though (the card says "and/or")

Subject: Re: Operatives Are Your Friends (ARTICLE)

From: Joe R. Golowka

Click Here!

swccg-discussion: Re: Operatives Are Your Friends (ARTICLE)[address removed] wrote:
> 
> What in the world is a straw man?

A common logical fallacy. It's when you misrepresent someone else's
position so that it can be attacked more easily, knock down that
misrepresented position, then conclude that the original position has
been demolished. It's a fallacy because it fails to deal with the actual
arguments that have been made.

Among his other misrepresentations, he claimed that those opposed to Ops
only wanted M&T decks to be dominant and then attacked that position. 
However, none of us ever claimed that so his attacks on it have no
bearing on the arguement.

-- 
Joe R. Golowka

Subject: Re: A History of SWCCG

From: Joe R. Golowka

Click Here!

swccg-discussion: Re: A History of [address removed] wrote:
> 
> >DAGOBAH.
> >
> >Whoa nellie! Back up the Asteroid train! Apparantly the playtesters at
> >Decipher were asleep during the Asteroid Sanctuary testing. It set the
> game
> >back by leaps and bounds. Plus, the Dark side had it's equally abusable
> >entity: Shot in the Dark.
> 
> Shot In The Dark was not an equally abuseable entity. The necessary combo's
> for Shot In The Dark nasty decks took a LONG time to arrive. Even the
> infinitely clever Manipulator from Michael Riboulet's '97 WC hadn't noticed
> any potential in this card.
> 
> The BOMB deck was one at that time (using SITD to force multiple
> Fear/Limited Resources per turn) but didn't quite have the necessary
> publicity or success rate to force an errata.

Yeah it did. They errata'd Limited Rescources, remember? Of course,
they kinda errata'd the wrong card but they didn't know that at the
time.

> In the end, it was CC Dark
> Deal + SITD that forced a rethink. Personally, I don't see how Decipher
> could have really seen that one coming, it was a pretty clever deck.

Actually, I believe it was the lock down deck which prevented your
opponet from activating any force which played the primary role in
getting it errata'd. That deck had no place in the game and it wouldv'e
been near-impossible to make an effective counter card for it. The
origonal article they posted on their page announing the errata (see:
http://www.decipher.com/insidedecipher/archives/1998/erratastory.htm to
read it again) stated that the errata was because of a strategy which
would lock the opponet.

> >So in conclusion, JP and SE have sent SWCCG into a tailspin while leading
> it
> >forward at the same time. The negative are overcoming the positive though.
> And
> >it's not good. Comments. Please. :-)

No way. JP was one of the best expansions wev'e ever had. It finally
cleaned up most of the non-interactions cause by Dagobah and made many
formerly useless cards usefull. There were a few problems, but compared
to other expansions it was awsome. SE, however, appears to be the next
generation of Dagobah decks. Poorly playtested with broken & degenerate
strategies.

> And let's face it - if Decipher choose a worthy fix to the Operative
> problem, then the set will still look pretty damn flash!

It'd look a lot better. But there are a couple other strategies which
may become problems (Hidden Base comes to mind). Still, errataing Ops
would improve the game by a lot.

>Okay, here's what you do:
>
>Turn one: If you can, deploy SoS to Yoda's Hut (deploy that too). Deploy
>all the Portable Scanners you can on him (no more than 5 though). The
>next step is to get an Encampment, but you probably can't afford that
>now.
>
>Turn two: Use Portable Scanner to get Luke's Backpack. Get and deploy:
>the Training Area, Great Warrior, Lukes Pack and Yoda. If you can, Signal
>for an Encampment. 
>
>Turn three: Attempt Jedi Test (need 4 or higher to pass). Use each
>Scanner to get as many Scanners into your hand as possible. Deploy
>Scanners until you have 5 on Luke. You should have an Encampment by now
>so deploy it along with all the ASP droids you can (but no more than 5).
>
>Turn four: Use the Scanners to search for any remaining Scanners in your
>deck. You should have 5 in your hand. Use the ASP droids to move all 5 of
>the scanners to the top of your Reserve Deck. Deploy your 5 Scanners to
>Luke. Now you have five 6 destiny cards on the top of your Reserve deck.
>The only problem with this strategy is that if you don't use all the
>scanners that you put on top of your pile, you are forced to activate
>them (I was thinking about putting in a Hydorponics Station and Vaporator
>in for that). 
>
>Turn five: Get Scanners, Put Scanners on Reserve, Deploy Scanners. Use
>Suprise Assaults and On The Edge to start getting back into the game. You
>can also spread out pretty easily, just as long as you have 4 ability at
>each site. If for some reason, you activate a Scanner, you'll have to
>play with only four 6's on top for a few turns (darn)
>
>What do you think? Can you think of any ways to make it faster? Also, it
>really should have a Hydro Station and Vaporator just incase you are
>forced to activate some of your Scanners. Also, some Tunnel Visions would
>help this. Jedi Levitations would be great too, and would really speed
>things up.

have this, it would slow them dow (very slightly but any is better thatn none)

Also, I dont know if any one has thought of this yet (probably have but her
goes any way)

To combat LS Ops

To counter the speed of the ops deck: start with JP:ETE.
 I realize that if this becomes common LS will start packing Uh-Oh's, but
we can deal with that later.

Also we need something to counter the strength of an Operative on its own
planet. Like say another Operative! Since you have JP sites out use JP
Sabbac to try and steal thier operatives then use it against them!

I realise this by it self would not beat the ops deck, but perhaps we can
figure something out, based on this.

PS About the JP:ETE/Uh-oh. Ops decks might start packing Uh-ohs (to counter
the ETE. But that is one less spot for an operative, or backup or something
else they might need. If we can come up with enough of these types of
ideas, they will have to pack the counters for ALL of them, thereby leaving
less room for "helpful" stuff. And thats a good thing. :)

From: Mark Tuttle
Subject: Re: A quick question on Starting Interrupts
Wayback: https://web.archive.org/web/19991103205622/http://www.egroups.com:80/group/swccg-discussion/67549.html?

Click Here!

swccg-discussion: Re: A quick question on Starting Interrupts> Can you use "Don't Tread On Me" 3 times after you declare your starting
> location to pull all 3 of the effects listed on your card to get them out of
> your reserve deck, then can you use "The Signal" to get another effect of your
> choice out?

You're not actually using DTOM 3 times. You may pull all three of those 
Effects out with one play. However, you may NOT use more than one 
starting interrupt at the beginning of the game.

Subject: Re: Wise Advice/There Is No Try

From: Douglas L. Faust

Click Here!

swccg-discussion: Re: Wise Advice/There Is No [address removed] wrote:

> 

> Ok, I have Wise Advice out on my side of the table, my opponent has There Is

> No try. We have a sense/alter war. Do my senses and alters go the the lost

> pile because of There Is No Try or do they go to the Used Pile because of Wise

> Advice?

Any cards that were canceled by Sense or Alter go to the Used Pile. Any

Senses or Alters that successfully went through go to the Lost Pile.

Weird, huh? The loser gets to keep all of his Senses and Alters, while

the winner loses them all. :-)

--

Subject: Do this!

From: tal-

Click Here!

swccg-discussion: Do this!I think this would be funny if it worked :P

I somehow doubt it will tho. :\

hey all. i know some of us hate mtv, some of us dislike it, some don't

care, some love it, and some just like to cause mischief. well here's

a way to do just that. please read what ive coppied here and follow

the directions. it'd be really cool if it worked...send it to all your

friends

Now, to expose the plot, a nefarious scheme I've contrived to pay them

all back. The idea struck me- like a truck running into a flightless

bird who had inadvertantly chosen to cross the road- while I was

watching TRL yesterday. TRL, for those of you who don't know, is the

abbreviation for the showcase of Carson Daly witticisms that is Total

Request Live on MTV (MTV itself being an abbreviation for music

television).

Here is what we must all pull together and do: send this email to as

many people as possible. The message is simple. On March 10, 1999,

everyone who has received this email will get on-line and, before the

airing of Total Request Live, cast their vote for the New Kids on the

Block's epic music video-"Hanging Tough." It is the ultimate insult

to popular culture.

And here is the compelling, dramatic, conclusion: I don't know if

this is original. I don't know if anyone has ever had this thought

before. You won't be cursed with bad luck and misfortune if you choose

not to pass this on. You, in turn, will not reap lifelong

rewards and benefits if you do. But you will laugh your ass off if it

works. And in that hysterical fit of laughter you will live the long

fruitful life and die the ill-fated tragic death all at the same time.

We can make this happen. Now the author would like to apologize for

any misspellings, offensive or politically incorrect ethnic slurs,

verbosity, inapropriate word use, and failure to convey a lucid,

well-organized thought.

Sincerely,

James Vaughan

P.S. Be sure to include ridiculous things in your request, such as:

"Please Carson, will you play "Hangin' Tough" by the New Kids on the

Block. Danny is such a clean-cut boy, and he'll never be in trouble

with the law. Also, I think that these guys have the Right Stuff,

and they're going to be around for a long time.

Good fun,

Josh
